     8	and which checks are fail-closed. Every conclusion the product surfaces carries **credibility and
     9	limitations** at minimum; most also carry **confidence** and **provenance**. The remaining fields — what

    12	`blockedReasons[]` (R3.0C credibility contract), `cannotConcludeReasonCodes[]` + `evidenceSummary[]` +

    17	
    18	The credibility ladder used throughout is:
    19	
    20	> **Physics > Model > Measured > Derived > Heuristic > Unavailable.**
    21	
    22	`Unavailable` is not a failure mode the UI hides — it is a named outcome with a machine-readable reason code.

    25	
    26	## Topology
    27	
    28	R3.0 is a single Electron app that is **also** a regular browser page. The two environments share one production
    29	tree and one persistence contract; the host differences are confined to a small Electron boundary.
    30	
    31	```
    32	+----------------------------------- Electron Host (main.js / preload.js) -----------------------------------+
    33	|  - contextIsolation: true, nodeIntegration: false explicitly set; no unsafe flag ever flipped on            |
    34	|  - preload exposes EXACTLY { platform, version } on window.electronAPI — no IPC, no FS, no shell            |
    35	|  - File:// origin loads renderer/index.html; CSP default-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval'           |
    36	+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
    37	            |
    38	            v
    39	+--------------------------------------- Renderer / Production Tree ------------------------------------------+
    40	|                                                                                                             |
    41	|  R3.0A SHELL                                                                                                |
    42	|     renderer/index.html  +  case-shell.js  (Alpine view-model + feature registry navigation)                |
    43	|       - non-reactive caseDataHolder for raw / large state (sessions, imported bundles)                      |
    44	|       - per-case nav availability re-derived from the capability the services compute                       |
    45	|                                                                                                             |
    46	|  R3.0B PERSISTENCE  (renderer/js/r3-0b-*.js)                                                                |
    47	|     case-store, session-store, case-record-schema, schema-migration, storage-backend, case-library-vm       |
    48	|       - IndexedDB in browser/Electron; MemoryBackend in Node tests                                          |
    49	|       - atomic transact({stores, reads, compute})  — one readwrite txn per mutation                         |
    50	|                                                                                                             |
    51	|  R3.0C COMPARISON AUTHORITY  (contracts/r3.0c/* + renderer/js/r3-0c-*.js)                                   |
    52	|     C2 lap + track identity                                                                                 |
    53	|     C3 normalized distance authority                                                                        |
    54	|     C4 reference selection (EXPLICIT user only) + corner segmentation + corner pairing                      |
    55	|     C5 delta metrics (allowlisted; sign = comparison - reference)                                           |
    56	|     C6 comparison export envelope                                                                           |
    57	|     C7 comparison workspace view model                                                                      |
    58	|     C8 activation (Feature Registry wiring)                                                                 |
    59	|                                                                                                             |
    60	|  R3.0D DECISION ENGINE  (contracts/r3.0d/* + renderer/js/r3-0d-*.js)                                        |
    61	|     D2 evidence-graph builder (closure-private WeakSet authority)                                           |
    62	|     D3 hypothesis engine (authoritative-only inputs)                                                        |
    63	|     D4 priority engine                                                                                      |
    64	|     D5 engineer brief generator                                                                             |
    65	|                                                                                                             |
    66	|  R3.0E EXPERIMENT / OUTCOME / TIMELINE / FOLLOW-UP STORES  (contracts/r3.0e/* + renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js)|
    67	|     experiment-store, outcome-store, timeline-store, followup-link-store, outcome-classifier, viewmodel     |
    68	|       - independent versioned IndexedDB namespaces (r3_0e_*)                                                |
    69	|       - append-only property is PER STORE (timeline-store), not per phase — see §R3.0E                      |
    70	|                                                                                                             |
    71	|  R3.0F INTEGRATED DELIVERY                                                                                  |
    72	|     F1 r3-0f-migration-engine.js  (sanitize / structured-clone firewall / trap-free serializer / atomic txn)|
    73	|     F2 e2e-harness               (9 flows: tests/e2e/flow-01..09-*.test.js)                                 |
    74	|     F3 hardening probes          (6 probes: tests/e2e/hardening-01..06-*.test.js)                           |
    75	|                                                                                                             |
    76	+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
    77	```
    78	
    79	A few invariants are global to the topology:
    80	
    81	- **One process tree, two hosts.** The same module set is loaded under Electron and under a plain browser tab.
    82	  No code path is conditional on `process` / `window.require` / `node:` — the shell never touches Node APIs.
    83	- **No runtime LLM.** No phase calls a model at runtime. The decision engine, classifier, and brief generator
    84	  are deterministic transforms of authoritative-only inputs.
    85	- **No cloud, no multi-user, no telemetry beacon.** Persistence is local-first; nothing is
    86	  auto-uploaded and the portable case bundle never carries raw telemetry. Raw telemetry CAN
    87	  leave the device, but only through the explicit, opt-in `exportRawArchive(sessionId)` call
    88	  (`session-store`, see below) — a separate user action from the case-bundle export.
    89	- **Feature Registry is the navigation truth source.** A capability is reachable only if its feature ID is
    90	  registered and `featureRegistryActivationAllowed` is `true` **for its own phase's governance state**. This
    91	  is a per-phase flag, not one train-wide switch: R3.0C (`C8_ACTIVATION`), R3.0D
    92	  (`D5_ENGINEER_BRIEF_ACTIVATION`), and R3.0E (`E5_ACTIVATION`) have each already flipped their own flag to
    93	  `true` — the Comparisons pane, the Engineer Brief pane, and the Experiment Loop / Case Timeline panes are
    94	  live in the shipped registry today. Only R3.0F's own flag remains `false` until `F6_RELEASE` (R3.0F has no
    95	  case-scoped pane of its own to gate).
    96	
    97	---
    98	
    99	## Storage backends + R3.0B persistence
   100	
   101	R3.0B is the layer that turns an Analysis Case from a transient view model into a **persistable, reopenable,
   102	duplicable, archivable** local-first engineering record. Browser and Electron share one contract.
   103	
   104	### Backend abstraction (`storage-backend.js`)
   105	
   106	A namespaced async KV with one atomic primitive:
   107	
   108	| API | Behaviour |
   109	| --- | --- |
   110	| `get(ns, key)` | Returns the stored value, or `undefined`. Never throws on missing. |
   111	| `put(ns, key, value)` | Overwrite. |
   112	| `add(ns, key, value)` | Create-if-absent; aborts on id collision. |
   113	| `del(ns, key)` | Idempotent. |
   114	| `list(ns)` | Returns the full `[{ key, value }]` array for the namespace. No `opts` parameter, no cursor/pagination API. |
   115	| `estimateBytes(ns)` | Best-effort size accounting for quota probes. |
   116	| `update(ns, key, fn)` | Single-key compare-and-set helper built atop `transact`. |
   117	| `transact({ stores, reads, compute })` | All reads declared up-front; `compute(readValues)` is **synchronous and pure**; returns the writes + a result that are applied in ONE `readwrite` transaction. Commit-or-abort; quota → typed `STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`. |
   118	
   119	Two implementations:
   120	
   121	- **`MemoryBackend`** — used by Node tests and any SSR-shaped environment. A global mutex + structured-clone at
   122	  every boundary guarantees the test backend behaves like IndexedDB w.r.t. clone semantics and concurrency.
   123	- **`IndexedDBBackend`** — used in the browser and Electron renderer. There is no silent fallback to memory:
   124	  if IndexedDB is unavailable, the layer surfaces `STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE` and the UI blocks the relevant capability
   125	  with a reason. A renderer that "looks empty" because IndexedDB silently degraded is exactly the failure mode
   126	  this contract refuses.
   127	
   128	### Case record + portable bundle (`case-record-schema.js`)
   129	
   130	Two strictly different shapes:
   131	
   132	- **Local record** — full sanitized analysis view model. `sanitizeForStorage` is **all-or-nothing**: JSON-safe;
   133	  cyclic / non-plain / function values are rejected; bounded enumeration and size caps are enforced. Any node
   134	  that violates → the save is **rejected**, never lossily stored. Raw telemetry sample arrays can never be
   135	  written even locally — they exceed the bounds by construction.
   136	- **Portable bundle** — a curated, strictly-allowlisted, value-constrained closed schema with enum vocabularies,
   137	  code/id patterns, and length caps. Any off-allowlist or constraint-failing field is **excluded and logged**;
   138	  required fields are validated; a future bundle version is rejected fail-closed. An imported bundle is stored
   139	  as an explicitly-degraded `imported_summary` and is **never** promoted into a full local analysis.
   140	
   141	### Schema migration (`schema-migration.js`)
   142	
   143	`migrateCaseRecord` / `migrateSessionRecord` migrate older versions step-by-step. A **future** schema version is
   144	**rejected fail-closed**, never coerced. Unknown fields on an older record are preserved on the local side
   145	(silently dropping a field is treated as evidence loss). Migration is deterministic and produces a logged trace.
   146	
   147	**Frozen v1.4.0 baseline.** The R3.0B `case-record-schema` was frozen at v1.4.0 and **has not been modified
   148	through R3.0F**. Every downstream phase (D / E / F) carries an explicit `r3bCaseRecordSchemaUntouched: true`
   149	governance assertion. R3.0E experiment / outcome / timeline / follow-up state lives in **independent versioned
   150	IndexedDB namespaces** (`r3_0e_*`), not on the case record.
   151	
   152	### Stores
   153	
   154	| Store | Namespace | Notes |
   155	| --- | --- | --- |
   156	| `case-store` | `cases` + `caseIndex` (one atomic txn) | `create / save / open / duplicate / archive / unarchive / setPinned / remove(confirm:true) / list / exportCase / importBundle / compact`. `save`-update requires an existing entry — a deleted case is **not** resurrected. `delete` is fail-closed without `confirm:true`. |
   157	| `session-store` | separate namespace | Raw telemetry is **byte-bounded** (`maxSessionBytes` / `maxRawBytes`); oldest-evicted with a bounded eviction log embedded in the index envelope so index/log can't diverge. Never auto-uploaded. Never in a portable case bundle. `exportRawArchive(id)` is an explicit opt-in distinct from the case export. |
   158	| `experiment-store` | `r3_0e_experiments` + `r3_0e_experimentsIndex` | R3.0E — **mutable** (`create / update / get / list / remove`). See §R3.0E. |
   159	| `outcome-store` | `r3_0e_outcomes` + `r3_0e_outcomesIndex` | R3.0E — API surface is `create / get / listForExperiment` (no update or remove API exposed). |
   160	| `timeline-store` | `r3_0e_timelines` | R3.0E — **append-only** per-case event document. See §R3.0E. |
   161	| `followup-link-store` | `r3_0e_followupLinks` + `r3_0e_followupLinksByCase` (reverse index) | R3.0E — `create / get / listForParent / markParentStatus`. **Not** append-only. |
   162	| `store-metadata` | `r3_0e_storeMetadata` (key `__r3_0e_version`) | Per-namespace schema-version map: `experiment / outcome / timeline / followUpLink → 1`. |
   163	
   164	### Honesty boundary
   165	
   166	Persistence stores and restores the **services' outputs**. It never recomputes physics, never re-decides
   167	eligibility, and never upgrades trust. Library indicators (telemetry-present, calibration-present, corner-eligible)
   168	read **stored evidence** — they do not re-run the pipeline against the saved record.
   169	
   170	---
   171	
   172	## R3.0C — Comparison authority pipeline
   173	
   174	R3.0C turns "two laps on the same track" into a defensible comparison. Every step here is
   175	**authority, not presence**: a field merely existing on a lap is never treated as authoritative; eligibility is
   176	re-derived from raw evidence on every run.
   177	
   178	```
   179	session laps  ─►  C2 lap authority ─►  C2 track identity  ─►  C3 normalized distance
   180	                                                                      │
   181	                                                                      v
   182	                       C4 explicit reference selection  ─►  C4 corner segmentation
   183	                                                                      │
   184	                                                                      v
   185	                                              C4 corner pairing  ─►  C5 delta metrics
   186	                                                                      │
   187	                                                                      v
   188	                                            C6 comparison export envelope  /  C7 view model
   189	                                                                      │
   190	                                                                      v
   191	                                                              C8 Feature Registry activation
   192	```
   193	
   194	### C2 — Lap and track identity
   195	
   196	A lap is **valid** only if all five authorities agree:
   197	
   198	1. **Lap identity** — a stable, declared lap id (no inference from filename / case title / channel name).
   199	2. **Completeness** — the lap covers a full normalized lap window per its declared lap-distance authority.
   200	3. **Timing validity** — non-monotonic timebase, gaps exceeding policy, or zero-duration laps → blocked.
   201	4. **Track identity** — a declared track identity for the session. Track is **never inferred** from titles,
   202	   filenames, or channel names.
   203	5. **Sample continuity** — sample density and gap policies are evaluated against the supplied policy.
   204	
   205	Any failure produces a frozen reason code (one of the 55+ in the R3.0C reason-code table) and no value.
   206	
   207	### C3 — Normalized distance
   208	
   209	The single authority for "where on a lap" is **lap-distance**. The C3 layer translates a declared lap-distance
   210	series into a `[0, 1)` normalized axis after exhausting a 16-code refusal list (empty input, single sample, NaN /
   211	Infinity, unsupported unit, unknown direction, non-monotonic in a non-wrap region, multiple wraps, insufficient
   212	samples / coverage, gap too large in normalized or time domain, extrapolation requested, identity mismatch,
   213	forged authority …). A series that fails any one is **blocked**, never smoothed.
   214	
   215	GPS, lap-time fraction, sample index, and other proxies are not accepted as distance authority. They may exist
   216	as channels; they are not promoted to a normalized axis.
   217	
   218	### C4 — Reference selection, segmentation, pairing
   219	
   220	**Reference selection is EXPLICIT USER SELECTION only.** There is no `fastest_valid`, no `median_valid`, no
   221	`best_sector_composite`, no auto-fallback. The user picks a reference lap by id; absent a selection, the
   222	comparison is **blocked with a reason**, not silently filled. This is a frozen scope-pin enforced by the train
   223	validator.
   224	
   225	**Corner segmentation** uses the normalized-distance axis and declared corner descriptors (when available) to
   226	partition each lap into corner windows. Segmentation is fail-closed: a degenerate or absent descriptor leaves
   227	the lap **unsegmented for that corner**, never approximated.
   228	
   229	**Corner pairing** pairs reference corners to comparison corners by normalized-distance identity, not by index
   230	or by name. A missing corner in one lap reduces aggregate coverage; below the minimum coverage threshold this
   231	is recorded as the limitation `CORNER_PAIRING_PARTIAL_COVERAGE` (non-blocking — downstream consumers see the
   232	limitation). Other corner-pairing failure modes have their own codes:
   233	`CORNER_PAIRING_UNAVAILABLE`, `CORNER_PAIRING_INSUFFICIENT_OVERLAP`, `CORNER_PAIRING_AMBIGUOUS`, and
   234	`CORNER_PAIRING_ORDINAL_FORBIDDEN`. There is no `CORNER_PAIRING_UNRESOLVED` code.
   235	
   236	### C5 — Delta metrics
   237	
   238	Allowlisted, signed metrics only:
   239	
   240	| Metric | Sign convention | Credibility | Blocked when |
   241	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
   242	| `lap_time` (lap scope) | comparison − reference | Derived | Lap time non-finite (`DELTA_METRIC_NUMERIC_INVALID`) |
   243	| `delta_cumulative` (lap scope; sum of paired-corner full-time deltas, `lap_time` fallback with zero pairs) | comparison − reference | Derived | No computable pair — partial pairing coverage surfaces a limitation, never a re-derived total |
   244	| `sector_delta` (per paired corner) | comparison − reference | Derived | Zero pairs (`DELTA_METRIC_CORNER_PAIR_REQUIRED`) / non-finite zone time for that pair (`DELTA_METRIC_NUMERIC_INVALID`) |
   245	| `entry_delta` / `mid_delta` / `exit_delta` (per paired corner, phase scope) | comparison − reference | Derived | Same as `sector_delta`, plus missing phase-boundary authorisation |
   246	
   247	(These six names are the closed `SUPPORTED_DELTA_METRICS` allowlist in `contracts/r3.0c/delta-metrics-contract.js`. Speed deltas are explicitly deferred at the C6 export layer, and the steering/throttle/brake input-delta names exist only in the CP1 eligibility vocabulary — `comparison-eligibility-contract.js`'s `SUPPORTED_METRICS`, which "computes none of them" — not in the C5 computation.)
   248	
   249	`delta = comparison − reference` is the **single convention**. The UI may never swap operands; the export
   250	envelope carries the operand order verbatim. No metric reports a magnitude where any upstream gate is open.
   251	
   252	### C6 / C7 — Export envelope and workspace view model
   253	
   254	The comparison export envelope carries a fixed identity string `racing-analyzer/comparison-export`, distinct
   255	from the R3.0B case-export schemas. The envelope validator **forbids raw / oversized arrays by construction**;
   256	an envelope that fails validation cannot be emitted. The comparison view model assembled at C7 binds only to
   257	service outputs; it never recomputes a delta, never re-decides eligibility, and surfaces every blocked metric
   258	with its reason code.
   259	
   260	### C8 — Activation
   261	
   262	The Feature Registry's `case_comparison` / `reference_lap` / `corner_delta` IDs are registered with renderer
   263	adapters at C8. The activation is gated by `featureRegistryActivationAllowed` in `governance/r3.0c/state.json`
   264	— and that flag is **already `true`** (`C8_ACTIVATION`). The Comparisons surface is live in the registry
   265	today (`availability: 'available'`, `rendererAdapter.paneId: 'comparisons'`, no `deferredReason`); it is not
   266	deferred. R3.0F's own, separate `featureRegistryActivationAllowed` flag remains `false` until F6, but that
   267	flag governs R3.0F's own (nonexistent) pane, not the already-activated R3.0C surface.
   268	
   269	---
   270	
   271	## R3.0D — Decision engine pipeline
   272	
   273	R3.0D consumes authoritative R3.0C output (and the case context) and produces a structured **Engineer Brief**.
   274	It does **not** run a model, does **not** call an LLM, does **not** claim causation from correlation, and does
   275	**not** blame the driver.
   276	
   277	```
   278	authoritative case context  +  C5 delta metrics  +  observation
   279	                              │
   280	                              v
   281	                 D2 evidence-graph builder   (closure-private WeakSet authority)
   282	                              │
   283	                              v
   284	                 D3 hypothesis engine        (authoritative-only inputs verified)
   285	                              │
   286	                              v
   287	                 D4 priority engine          (deterministic ordering)
   288	                              │
   289	                              v
   290	                 D5 engineer brief           (frozen export; deep-frozen graph)
   291	```
   292	
   293	### D2 — Evidence graph
   294	
   295	The evidence-graph builder ingests **only** values that pass authority verification. Once the graph is fully
   296	validated, sanitized, and deep-frozen, the single materialized `graph` object is registered once in a
   297	**closure-private WeakSet** (`_authoritativeGraphs`) — not per node; the WeakSet's only members are whole graph
   298	objects, one per successful `buildEvidenceGraph` call. Downstream stages verify graph identity through a
   299	`verifyAuthoritativeGraph` helper (checking WeakSet membership of the candidate graph object itself, plus
   300	structural checks such as `schemaVersion`, `graphId`, and `Array.isArray(candidate.nodes)`) before reading any
   301	field. A graph object constructed outside the builder cannot re-enter the pipeline — the WeakSet identity check
   302	rejects it.
   303	
   304	The graph carries an explicit `LIMITATION_IMPORTED_SUMMARY` propagation: a case opened from an imported bundle
   305	carries the limitation forward through D2 → D3 → D5 so the Brief can't silently upgrade an `imported_summary`
   306	into a full analysis.
   307	
   308	`Array.isArray` (and other type predicates) are **captured at module load** so a later prototype tamper cannot
   309	shift authority. The exported graph object is deep-frozen; the module's browser global is installed via
   310	`Object.defineProperty` to refuse silent reassignment.
   311	
   312	### D3 — Hypothesis engine
   313	
   314	Hypotheses are derived deterministically from the verified graph. Each hypothesis carries the credibility ladder
   315	rung supporting it (typically Derived or Heuristic for hypotheses, Physics or Model for the inputs cited as
   316	evidence), the evidence references it depends on, and the limitations attached.
   317	
   318	Forbidden hypothesis shapes:
   319	
   320	- A causal claim from a correlation. Hypotheses use "consistent with" / "would be reduced by" framing; never
   321	  "caused by".
   322	- A driver-attribution claim about the vehicle. Driver behaviour ≠ vehicle characteristic ≠ setup finding.
   323	- A magnitude that requires calibration the case does not carry. A hypothesis about understeer never quotes
   324	  measured K_us absent the canonical 5-condition prerequisite set (see
   325	  [r3-credibility-model.md](./r3-credibility-model.md) — same-case steady-state segment + verified road-wheel
   326	  steering calibration + lateral-acceleration channel verified + speed channel verified + minimum sample / range
   327	  coverage); it falls back to a directional tendency. Likewise a hypothesis about roll gradient never quotes a
   328	  measured deg/g value absent a verified suspension installation ratio and a verified roll-rate channel — it
   329	  remains a directional tendency, not a magnitude.
   330	
   331	### D4 — Priority engine
   332	
   333	A deterministic ordering of D3 hypotheses by a fixed scoring contract (evidence count, credibility rung,
   334	limitation severity, comparison validity). The ordering is reproducible across runs given the same inputs; it
   335	does **not** weight by user history, "interestingness", or any opaque signal. A tie-break order is declared and
   336	covered by tests.
   337	
   338	### D5 — Engineer brief
   339	
   340	The Brief is the structured authoritative output: ordered hypotheses, evidence references, limitations,
   341	blockers, comparison validity status, and a deferred-evidence list. It is **frozen** on emit; the consumer
   342	cannot mutate it. A blocked Brief (e.g. comparison-invalid upstream) carries a reason code and no hypotheses;
   343	an empty hypothesis list is not coerced into a "no issues" claim.
   344	
   345	The Brief carries a generation token bound to the inputs; a stale Brief cannot be replayed against a different
   346	case. Token-poisoning, retired-token replay, and blocked-prepare poisoning are explicit closure-tested
   347	adversarial cases.
   348	
   349	### What R3.0D refuses to be
   350	
   351	- It is not a recommendation engine that applies a setup. No code path mutates a setup record from a Brief.
   352	- It is not a measurement engine. Every magnitude in a Brief came from upstream services with their own
   353	  credibility metadata, which the Brief carries forward verbatim.
   354	- It is not a professional race-engineer replacement. This is a product-level positioning statement, not a
   355	  Brief `limitations[]` entry: the closed `LIMITATION_*` reason-code enum (`contracts/r3.0d/reason-codes.js`)
   356	  has no code for it, and `buildEngineerBrief`'s `limitations` union (`renderer/js/r3-0d-engineer-brief.js`)
   357	  only ever admits values that pass `RC.isReasonCode`. The equivalent guarantee is enforced structurally
   358	  instead: the causal-overclaim lexical scanner (`contracts/r3.0d/hypothesis-contract.js`
   359	  `CAUSAL_OVERCLAIM_TERMS`, which includes `professional_diagnosis`) rejects the whole record with
   360	  `HYPOTHESIS_CAUSAL_OVERCLAIM` if a Brief's text ever claims professional-diagnosis authority.
   361	- **Setup-related recommendations are emitted in physical units (Nm/deg, N/mm, mm, %) only. Hardware-click
   362	  counts are never emitted — no validated per-car click-to-rate mapping exists.** A user mapping clicks to
   363	  physical rates is a manual, out-of-engine step.
   364	
   365	---
   366	
   367	## R3.0E — Experiment / outcome / timeline / follow-up stores
   368	
   369	R3.0E records what the user **did about** a Brief: ran an experiment, observed an outcome, walked the case
   370	timeline, scheduled a follow-up to another case. The four stores live in **independent versioned IndexedDB
   371	namespaces** and are implemented in `renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js`. The module header is explicit about the
   372	boundary:
   373	
   374	> R3.0E persistence lives in SEPARATE versioned stores and MUST NOT extend the frozen R3.0B portable
   375	> case-record schema body.
   376	
   377	Every payload is validated by its E1 contract **before write** inside `compute()`. Every payload reached
   378	through a single-record read (`get()`; the per-link reads inside `listForParent()`) is re-validated on read,
   379	and a future `schemaVersion` is rejected fail-closed — with one exception: `experimentStore.list()` and
   380	`outcomeStore.listForExperiment()` (`renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js:149`, `:213`) read directly from the
   381	lightweight index stores and return those values as-is, with no schema-version or shape check. Persisted
   382	records carry **no runtime authority** — the D2
   383	WeakSet identity is closure-private and cannot survive reload, so rehydration consumers must re-validate against
   384	the E1 contracts before treating any value as authoritative. Writes go through the same
   385	`backend.transact({ stores, reads, compute })` primitive as R3.0B.
   386	
   387	### Per-store mutability — the append-only property is per store, not per phase
   388	
   389	| Store | Mutation API | Append-only? | Cross-case allowed? |
   390	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
   391	| `experiment-store` (`r3_0e_experiments`) | `create / update / get / list / remove` | **No** | No |
   392	| `outcome-store` (`r3_0e_outcomes`) | `create / get / listForExperiment` (no `update` / `remove` exposed) | API surface is **append-only by omission**; the store does not advertise itself as append-only the way `timeline-store` does | No (bound to an experiment within the same case) |
   393	| `timeline-store` (`r3_0e_timelines`) | `getTimeline(caseId) / appendEvent(caseId, event)` | **Yes — strictly append-only** | No (per case) |
   394	| `followup-link-store` (`r3_0e_followupLinks` + `r3_0e_followupLinksByCase`) | `create / get / listForParent / markParentStatus` | **No** — `markParentStatus(linkId, newStatus)` mutates state | No cross-case authority (see below) |
   395	
   396	#### `experiment-store` — mutable, schema-guarded, stale-write-guarded
   397	
   398	Records are keyed by `experimentId`; the index is keyed by `experimentId` and carries
   399	`{ experimentId, sourceCaseId, status, createdAt, updatedAt }`. `update` enforces two guards:
   400	
   401	- `existing.schemaVersion > SCHEMA_VERSIONS.experiment` → reject with `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_FUTURE_SCHEMA`.
   402	- `existing.createdAt !== rec.createdAt` → reject with `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_STALE_WRITE`.
   403	
   404	Reason codes: `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_INVALID`, `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_SCHEMA_MISMATCH`, `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_ID_COLLISION`,
   405	`R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_MISSING`, `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_FUTURE_SCHEMA`, `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_STALE_WRITE`,
   406	`R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_CORRUPTED`.
   407	
   408	#### `outcome-store` — create-only API surface
   409	
   410	`outcomeStore.create / get / listForExperiment`. There is no `update` and no `remove` API exposed; in
   411	practice the API surface is append-only, but the module does not declare itself with the strict append-only
   412	invariants `timeline-store` uses. Reason codes: `R3_0E_OUTCOME_INVALID`, `R3_0E_OUTCOME_ID_COLLISION`,
   413	`R3_0E_OUTCOME_FUTURE_SCHEMA`, `R3_0E_OUTCOME_CORRUPTED`.
   414	
   415	#### `timeline-store` — the only strictly append-only store
   416	
   417	`timeline-store` is keyed per case (`r3_0e_timelines`); the document shape is
   418	`{ schemaVersion, caseId, events: [] }`. `appendEvent(caseId, event)` enforces, in order:
   419	
   420	- **Future `schemaVersion`** on the stored document → `R3_0E_TIMELINE_FUTURE_SCHEMA`.
   421	- **Duplicate `eventId`** within the same case → `R3_0E_TIMELINE_DUPLICATE_EVENT`.
   422	- **Out-of-order timestamp** — `Date.parse(event.createdAt) < Date.parse(lastEvent.createdAt)` (or NaN on
   423	  the new event's side) → `R3_0E_TIMELINE_OUT_OF_ORDER`. A NaN `createdAt` on the stored last event is not
   424	  caught by this guard; it surfaces as `R3_0E_TIMELINE_INVALID` via the re-validation step below.
   425	- **Re-validation** of the resulting document shape via `validateCaseTimelineShape` before write.
   426	
   427	A correction is therefore a **new event**, never a mutation of an existing event. Reason codes:
   428	`R3_0E_TIMELINE_FUTURE_SCHEMA`, `R3_0E_TIMELINE_CORRUPTED`, `R3_0E_TIMELINE_DUPLICATE_EVENT`,
   429	`R3_0E_TIMELINE_OUT_OF_ORDER`, `R3_0E_TIMELINE_INVALID`.
   430	
   431	#### `followup-link-store` — mutable parent-status, reverse-index integrity
   432	
   433	A link carries `linkId / parentCaseId / followUpCaseId / experimentId`. The reverse index is keyed by
   434	`parentCaseId` and stores a `linkIds[]` payload. `listForParent(parentCaseId)` re-validates membership on every
   435	read:
   436	
   437	- Reverse-index row's `parentCaseId` must equal the caller-supplied `parentCaseId`, otherwise
   438	  `R3_0E_LINK_CORRUPTED` `'reverse-index parentCaseId mismatch'`.
   439	- Reverse-index `linkIds` must be an array, otherwise `R3_0E_LINK_CORRUPTED` `'reverse-index linkIds not array'`.
   440	- Every fetched link's `parentCaseId` must equal the caller's `parentCaseId`, otherwise
   441	  `R3_0E_LINK_CORRUPTED` `'reverse-index points at link with mismatched parentCaseId'`.
   442	
   443	`markParentStatus(linkId, newStatus)` is a **mutation API**: the allowed statuses are exactly
   444	`'present' / 'archived' / 'deleted'`; any other value is rejected with `R3_0E_LINK_PARENT_STATUS_INVALID`. The
   445	follow-up link **never** carries comparison authority — cross-case comparison is forbidden by R3.0C.
   446	
   447	Reason codes: `R3_0E_LINK_INVALID`, `R3_0E_LINK_ID_COLLISION`, `R3_0E_LINK_FUTURE_SCHEMA`,
   448	`R3_0E_LINK_CORRUPTED`, `R3_0E_LINK_MISSING`, `R3_0E_LINK_PARENT_STATUS_INVALID`.
   449	
   450	#### `store-metadata` — per-namespace schema-version map
   451	
   452	`storeMetadata.readVersion() / writeVersion(versionMap)` against the metadata key `__r3_0e_version`. The
   453	authoritative schema versions are `{ experiment: 1, outcome: 1, timeline: 1, followUpLink: 1 }`.
   454	
   455	### Outcome classifier
   456	
   457	`r3-0e-outcome-classifier.js` (authoritative entry `classifyOutcome(input, opts)`) accepts a **closed five-key
   458	input wrapper** — `experiment`, `appliedChange`, `followUp` (same-case/same-session/explicit-reference/
   459	comparability attestation), `observation` (direction/magnitude/evidence ids), and
   460	`controlVariableObservations`. It never accepts a caller-provided `class`, `confounders`, or
   461	`comparabilityScore`. The Outcome output has exactly thirteen keys, matching
   462	`contracts/r3.0e/outcome-contract.js`'s `OUTCOME_KEYS`:
   463	
   464	```
   465	{ schemaVersion, outcomeId, experimentId, class, observedDirection, observedMagnitude,
   466	  comparabilityScore, confounders, driverFeedback, dataQualityIssues, sideEffects,
   467	  limitations, createdAt }
   468	```
   469	
   470	`class` is one of six values, ordered by precedence:
   471	
   472	1. **`invalid_comparison`** — cross-case, cross-session (`followUp.sessionId !== followUp.parentSessionId`),
   473	   no explicit reference, or `comparabilityScore < 0.5`. Takes precedence over every other class.
   474	2. **`inconclusive_due_to_confounders`** — a control variable declared in `experiment.controlVariables` is
   475	   missing from the observed set, or an observed one has `withinRange !== true`.
   476	3. **`inconclusive`** — `observation.dataQualityIssues.length` exceeds a fixed threshold (4), or
   477	   `observation.observedDirection === null`.
   478	4. **`contradicted`** — `observation.contradictingEvidenceIds` is non-empty, or the observed direction does
   479	   not match `experiment.expectedDirection`.
   480	5. **`confirmed`** — observed direction matches the expected direction AND `observedMagnitude` falls within
   481	   `experiment.expectedMagnitudeRange`.
   482	6. **`partially_confirmed`** (fallback) — observed direction matches but the magnitude falls outside the
   483	   expected range.
   484	
   485	There is no `refuted` class, no `cannotConclude` class, and no credibility-floor gate in this classifier —
   486	classification is driven entirely by the structural checks above. See `docs/r3-experiment-loop.md` "Outcome
   487	classes" for the full table with file:line grounding.
   488	
   489	### Honesty contract for the experiment loop
   490	
   491	The experiment + outcome + timeline + follow-up stores record intent and observation. They do **not** validate
   492	that a setup change "worked" beyond what the comparison authority and controlled-variable bookkeeping can
   493	support. A `confirmed` outcome is a structured statement that *the controlled change is consistent with the
   494	observed comparison delta within declared limitations* — not a measured performance gain.
   495	
   496	---
   497	
   498	## R3.0F — Migration engine, e2e flows, hardening probes
   499	
   500	R3.0F is three things in one phase: the migration engine that bridges persisted records to the runtime, the
   501	end-to-end flow harness that exercises the full product path, and the hardening probes that assert the
   502	fail-closed boundaries hold under pressure.
   503	
   504	### F1 — Migration engine
   505	
   506	The migration engine (`renderer/js/r3-0f-migration-engine.js`) is **deterministic**, **fail-closed**, and
   507	assumes nothing about the provenance of its input. The public factory is:
   508	
   509	```
   510	createMigrationEngine({ backend, registry?, metaStore?, journalKey?, stateKey?,
   511	                        clock?, stamp?, maxJournalEntries?, maxRecordBytes? })
   512	  -> { detect, plan, migrate, journal, envelope, knownStores }
   513	```
   514	
   515	- `detect()` → frozen `{ ok, currentEnvelope, targetEnvelope, envelopeMismatch, knownStores, perStoreStatus }` — `currentEnvelope` is always present, `null` when no prior migration state exists.
   516	- `plan()` → frozen `{ ok, generatedAt, steps, blockers, perStoreSummary, targetEnvelope, currentEnvelope }`.
   517	- `migrate({ confirm: true })` → frozen `{ ok, report | reasonCode }`. `confirm:true` is required;
   518	  any other value short-circuits with `CONFIRM_REQUIRED`.
   519	- `journal()` → frozen array of journal entries (most recent last).
   520	- `envelope()` → frozen target envelope.
   521	- `knownStores()` covers `cases`, `sessions`, `r3_0e_experiments`, `r3_0e_outcomes`, `r3_0e_timelines`,
   522	  `r3_0e_followupLinks`. (`detect()`'s return value also carries a separate `knownStores` array field with
   523	  the same contents.)
   524	
   525	Meta is persisted in the `meta` store under `__r3_0f_migration_journal__` (journal) and
   526	`__r3_0f_migration_state__` (state); journals retain `MAX_JOURNAL_ENTRIES_DEF = 256` entries (overflow factor
   527	4 → `JOURNAL_OVERFLOW`), and `maxJournalEntries` is overridable via constructor. Record-integrity hashes are
   528	FNV-1a 64 (non-cryptographic) per F1-R1-08.
   529	
   530	The engine is built from four layered defences, in order.
   531	
   532	#### 1. `_sanitize` — JSON-safe shape gate (actual limits)
   533	
   534	`_sanitize` is run on every record before it touches a downstream module. The actual structural limits, read
   535	from `renderer/js/r3-0f-migration-engine.js`, are:
   536	
   537	| Walker | Depth cap | Effect at cap |
   538	| --- | --- | --- |
   539	| `_isJsonSafe` | **256** | `if (depth > 256) return false;` — record rejected as not JSON-safe. |
   540	| `_isAccessorFreeDescriptorTree` | **256** | `if (depth > 256) return false;` — descriptor walk fails closed. |
   541	| `_safeJsonStringify` | **256** | `if (depth > 256) return null;` — serializer fails closed. |
   542	| `_containsAttestationField` | **64** | `if (depth > 64) return true;` — depth-bomb defence treats the input as attestation-suspect. |
   543	
   544	There is **no universal `Array.length ≤ 256` cap and no depth-12 cap** anywhere in `_sanitize` /
   545	`_isJsonSafe` / `_safeJsonStringify`. Arrays are walked element-by-element, bounded only by the depth-256
   546	ceiling above and by the byte cap below. The effective size ceiling is the byte cap:
   547	
   548	- `MAX_RECORD_BYTES_DEF = 8_000_000` (**8 MB**), enforced by `_safeJsonStringify` length against
   549	  the engine's captured `String.prototype` methods. Override via the `maxRecordBytes` constructor option.
   550	  Exceeding the cap → `RECORD_TOO_LARGE`.
   551	
   552	`_isJsonSafe` rejects:
   553	
   554	- `BigInt`, `function`, `symbol`,
   555	- `NaN` / `Infinity` (non-finite numbers),
   556	- `Date` / `Map` / `Set` / `RegExp` / typed arrays — any value whose prototype is not `Object.prototype` and
   557	  not `null`,
   558	- accessor descriptors and any descriptor lacking the `value` key (pre-clone walk via
   559	  `_isAccessorFreeDescriptorTree`),
   560	- `Proxy` inputs (caught by the structured-clone firewall below).
   561	
   562	`undefined` **on a property** is JSON-safe — it is stripped by JSON serialization per ECMA-262 §25.5.2, and
   563	the R3.0B `importBundle` already relies on this (`schema: undefined`).
   564	
   565	A sanitize failure is a typed error with a path pointer; the migration aborts, the existing record stays
   566	intact, and the UI surfaces a readable block. Sanitize reason codes: `RECORD_NOT_AN_OBJECT`,
   567	`PROXY_INPUT_REJECTED`, `RECORD_CIRCULAR`, `RECORD_TOO_LARGE`.
   568	
   569	#### 2. Structured-clone-only firewall
   570	
   571	After sanitize passes, every record crosses module boundaries through a **structured-clone-only firewall**.
   572	The engine refuses to construct at module load if `structuredClone` is unavailable, and rejects an input that
   573	`structuredClone` throws on (`PROXY_INPUT_REJECTED`). A caller cannot retain a live reference into the engine's
   574	internal graph and a producer cannot pass a captured prototype through. The clone semantics are exactly the
   575	ones `MemoryBackend` already used at every R3.0B boundary, lifted to a per-call contract.
   576	
   577	#### 3. Trap-free serializer
   578	
   579	When the engine has to emit a string (export envelopes, debug artifacts, integrity hashes), it routes through
   580	the **trap-free `_safeJsonStringify`** built on the closure-captured `Array.isArray`, captured
   581	`Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty`, captured `String.prototype` / `Number.prototype` methods invoked through
   582	`_ReflectApply`, and own-enumerable-key enumeration that ignores accessors. A `toJSON` method on an input is
   583	**not honoured** — the serializer is producer-attestation-defended: the producer does not get to decide what
   584	its own bytes look like. `JSON.stringify` is intentionally **not** used post-clone, to neutralize hostile
   585	`Object.prototype.toJSON` hooks added after engine load.
   586	
   587	The engine also defends a producer-attestation field-name allowlist. The 15 sentinel field names are:
   588	`_authoritative`, `_producerAttested`, `_attested`, `__attested`, `_verified`, `__verified`, `_signature`,
   589	`__signature`, `_proof`, `__proof`, `_authority`, `__authority`, `_authoritativeSession`, `_authoritativeCase`,
   590	`_authoritativeOutcome`. The 7 tokens are `authoritative`, `producerattested`, `attested`, `verified`,
   591	`signature`, `proof`, `authority`. A key is refused if its NFKC-lowercased form exactly matches a sentinel name
   592	**or** the key starts with `_` (normalized) and contains one of the tokens. Ordinary fields such as
   593	`lapAuthority` / `projectionSignature` / `experimentVerified` pass — the rule is a sentinel match, not a
   594	keyword scan. Defang is via the regex
   595	`/[\p{Default_Ignorable_Code_Point}\p{Mn}\p{Cc}\p{Cs}\p{White_Space}⠀]/gu`. A refused field surfaces
   596	`PRODUCER_ATTESTATION_REFUSED`. **F1 never fabricates an attestation field on its own output.**
   597	
   598	#### 4. Atomic `transact` with TOCTOU defence
   599	
   600	All migration writes go through the R3.0B `transact({ stores, reads, compute })` primitive. `reads` are
   601	declared up front; `compute` is synchronous and pure; the writes are committed or aborted as one. Per
   602	F1-R1-03, the commit covers the store writes AND the journal AND the migration state in **one atomic
   603	`backend.transact()`** across all stores + META — there is no in-between state where two writers can race.
   604	
   605	The migrator-result boundary further enforces an explicit closed enum: `ok` must be an explicit boolean;
   606	`migrationsApplied` must be an array of plain strings; `reason` must be one of the envelope reason codes (or
   607	mapped to `NO_MIGRATION_PATH`). The full envelope reason-code set is `UNSUPPORTED_FUTURE_VERSION`,
   608	`NO_MIGRATION_PATH`, `RECORD_NOT_AN_OBJECT`, `RECORD_BAD_VERSION`, `RECORD_TOO_LARGE`, `RECORD_CIRCULAR`,
   609	`PROXY_INPUT_REJECTED`, `POST_MIGRATION_INVALID`, `MIGRATOR_THREW`, `BACKEND_REJECTED`, `CONFIRM_REQUIRED`,
   610	`ENVELOPE_VERSION_MISMATCH`, `JOURNAL_OVERFLOW`, `PRODUCER_ATTESTATION_REFUSED`. Envelope `status` is one of
   611	`migrated / no-op / rejected / failed`.
   612	
   613	The migration engine is the first F-phase module with `runtimeConsumersAllowed = true` and
   614	`algorithmsAllowed = true`. R3.0F's own `featureRegistryActivationAllowed` remains `false` until F6 —
   615	the migration engine has no case-scoped pane of its own to gate. (This is separate from R3.0C/D/E's own
   616	activation flags, which have already flipped to `true` for their respective phases — see "Feature Registry
   617	is the navigation truth source" above.)
   618	
   619	### F2 — End-to-end flow harness (9 flows)
   620	
   621	Nine flows under `tests/e2e/flow-{01..09}-*.test.js` drive the production tree from cold start through
   622	import, comparison, brief, experiment, follow-up, and Electron smoke. The exact filenames and purposes are:
   623	
   624	| File | Purpose |
   625	| --- | --- |
   626	| `tests/e2e/flow-01-new-user.test.js` | New-user empty-state journey. A fresh `MemoryBackend()` instance (Node-only logic harness, not IndexedDB or a browser — see `tests/e2e/helpers/flow-harness.js:44`) produces a deterministic empty Case Library; F1 reports zero records; envelope at v1; forbidden actions (auto reference lap / auto setup apply / runtime-LLM authority / causation / driver blame) disabled. Zero console error. |
   627	| `tests/e2e/flow-02-real-telemetry.test.js` | Real telemetry import. |
   628	| `tests/e2e/flow-03-measured.test.js` | Measured-metrics flow. |
   629	| `tests/e2e/flow-04-reference-lap.test.js` | Reference-lap explicit selection (no auto fastest / median / best-sector composite). |
   630	| `tests/e2e/flow-05-vre.test.js` | VRE / R3.0D Engineer Brief on authoritative-only inputs. The brief does **not** classify, claim causation, blame the driver, or take runtime-LLM authority — it is a **read projection** of upstream services. |
   631	| `tests/e2e/flow-06-setup-experiment.test.js` | Setup experiment create + timeline append-only. Appends an `outcome_classified` timeline event directly (does not call `classifyOutcome`). A correction is a NEW timeline event, not a mutation of an existing one. |
   632	| `tests/e2e/flow-07-driver-experiment.test.js` | Driver experiment with follow-up Case link. Follow-up Case Links carry **no comparison authority** (cross-case forbidden). Attempts `followupLinkStore.create(link)`; does not exercise `listForParent` or `markParentStatus`. |
   633	| `tests/e2e/flow-08-export-import.test.js` | Case export + reimport. |
   634	| `tests/e2e/flow-09-electron-smoke.test.js` | Electron startup smoke. Reads `package.json` to confirm `electron` is a declared devDependency with a valid semver range; `main.js` has a stable entry shape; the preload exposes only the minimal `contextBridge` surface; no `nodeIntegration` leak. Does **not** invoke the `electron` binary, launch a window, or render UI. |
   635	
   636	### F3 — Hardening probes (6 probes)
   637	
   638	Six adversarial probes under `tests/e2e/hardening-{01..06}-*.test.js` (133 assertions across the suite)
   639	target the fail-closed boundaries identified earlier in this document:
   640	
   641	| File | Target |
   642	| --- | --- |
   643	| `tests/e2e/hardening-01-electron-boundary.test.js` | Electron preload surface is exactly `{ platform, version }`; no IPC channel, no FS, no shell spawn, no `webContents` exposure. Drift fails the gate. |
   644	| `tests/e2e/hardening-02-storage-failure.test.js` | `case-store.remove` requires explicit `confirm:true` (`CONFIRM_REQUIRED` otherwise); a `backend.transact` failure leaves the source record completely unchanged (`BACKEND_REJECTED`, status `halted`, no partial write); an oversized record is rejected by the record-bytes cap. |
   645	| `tests/e2e/hardening-03-no-stale-ui.test.js` | After a Case/Session transition, no viewmodel retains a stale case-id reference, across the full documented set of case-id-bearing fields (`lastSession.*`, `cachedCaseId`/`sourceCaseId`/`priorCaseId`/`parentCaseId`/`followUpCaseId`, R3.0C C8 `lastReassertion.caseId`, R3.0D `currentBrief.caseAssociation`, R3.0E `currentExperiment`/`currentOutcome`/`currentTimeline.caseAssociation`) — verified against a real R3.0E experiment record's production shape, not just a synthetic object. |
   646	| `tests/e2e/hardening-04-large-library.test.js` | The case-store + F1 migration engine scale **bounded-linear**, not quadratic, as library size grows — verified by counting `backend.list`/`get`/`transact` operations at `N` and `2N`. |
   647	| `tests/e2e/hardening-05-xss-injection.test.js` | Static scan confirming the renderer never pipes user-supplied or case-derived text into `innerHTML` or `document.write`. |
   648	| `tests/e2e/hardening-06-supply-chain.test.js` | `package.json` declares only the known Electron/electron-builder dependencies; no production renderer module pulls a bare third-party `require()`; no `package.json` script references untrusted tooling; no committed secrets/API keys/`.env` files. (The probe also checks for `CHANGELOG.md`'s presence, but that check is hardcoded non-fatal — `chk('CHANGELOG.md presence check is non-fatal at F3', true)` — and passes unconditionally regardless of whether the file exists.) |
   649	
   650	---
   651	
   652	## Electron host boundaries
   653	
   654	The Electron host is deliberately thin.
   655	
   656	```
   657	main.js       — creates a BrowserWindow; webPreferences below
   658	preload.js    — exposes exactly { platform, version } onto window.electronAPI
   659	renderer/     — runs as if it were a browser tab; never imports node:* / electron / fs / child_process
   660	```
   661	
   662	`main.js`'s `BrowserWindow` `webPreferences` object literal explicitly sets exactly three keys:
   663	
   664	| Option | Value | Why |
   665	| --- | --- | --- |
   666	| `preload` | path to `preload.js` | The only privileged surface. |
   667	| `contextIsolation` | `true` | Renderer cannot reach into the preload's realm. |
   668	| `nodeIntegration` | `false` | No `require` / `process` / `Buffer` in the renderer. |
   669	
   670	No other `webPreferences` key (`sandbox`, `webSecurity`, `allowRunningInsecureContent`,
   671	`nodeIntegrationInWorker`, `nodeIntegrationInSubFrames`, `enableRemoteModule`) is present in the object
   672	literal — they are absent, not explicitly hardened, and Electron's own defaults apply. The F3 boundary
   673	probe (`tests/e2e/hardening-01-electron-boundary.test.js`) does not assert these are explicitly set; it
   674	asserts `contextIsolation`/`nodeIntegration` are each declared exactly once with their safe literal value,
   675	and that none of the other keys is ever explicitly flipped to an unsafe value (e.g. `sandbox: false`,
   676	`webSecurity: false`, `nodeIntegration: true`) — a regression that adds an explicit unsafe flag fails
   677	closed; a contributor who never touches these keys does not.
   678	
   679	`renderer/index.html` declares `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval'`.
   680	This restricts the default fetch directive — and therefore `connect-src`, which falls back to `default-src`
   681	when unset — to the app's own origin, so no remote network destination is reachable from a CSP standpoint.
   682	It does **not** block inline `<script>` tags or `eval()` (`'unsafe-inline'`/`'unsafe-eval'` are explicitly
   683	allowed), and the policy has no `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'self'`, or `frame-ancestors 'none'`
   684	directive. The F3 probe only asserts `default-src 'self'` is present in the declared policy.
   685	
   686	The preload surface is **exactly** `{ platform, version }` exposed via `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld`
   687	under the name `electronAPI` (`window.electronAPI`). There is no `ipcRenderer.invoke` channel, no FS
   688	access, no shell spawn, no `webContents` exposure. A renderer-side feature that "would need a native API" is
   689	either solved without one or stays out of scope. The F3 boundary hardening probe
   690	(`hardening-01-electron-boundary.test.js`) verifies this no-IPC contract byte by byte against an expected
   691	schema and fails closed on any drift — a preload that grew an unexpected property would block the release
   692	gate.
   693	
   694	---
   695	
   696	## Determinism + fail-closed contracts
   697	
   698	### Determinism
   699	
   700	R3.0 production paths are deterministic by construction:
   701	
   702	- **No `Math.random` in analysis/decision paths.** Sampling, ordering, and pairing are deterministic. Where a
   703	  random source would be tempting (tie-breaking, jitter), an explicit declared rule replaces it. The sole
   704	  exception is opaque local storage key generation — `case-store.js`'s and `session-store.js`'s `_newId()`
   705	  helpers use `Math.random()` (mixed with `Date.now()`) to mint `caseId`/`sessionId` strings when the caller
   706	  does not supply one; these ids are never inputs to sampling, ordering, pairing, or any credibility/decision
   707	  computation, only opaque storage keys.
   708	- **No wall-clock dependence in decision logic.** Time values consumed by the decision engine come from
   709	  declared record timestamps, not `Date.now()`. The E2 experiment, outcome, follow-up-link, and timeline
   710	  stores validate and persist a caller-supplied `createdAt` string — they do not generate it at insertion
   711	  from the platform clock, and only the timeline store's `appendEvent` checks ordering, rejecting a
   712	  strictly-earlier `createdAt` with `R3_0E_TIMELINE_OUT_OF_ORDER` (equal timestamps are accepted; the E4
   713	  service `renderer/js/r3-0e-followup-timeline.js` is the one that generates clock-derived timestamps and
   714	  enforces strict monotonicity for its production entry points).
   715	- **No floating-point ordering drift.** Comparators carry an explicit tie-break rule; integer-key sort orders
   716	  are preferred where one is available.
   717	- **Captured intrinsics.** D2 captures `Array.isArray` and similar predicates at module load so a later tamper
   718	  cannot retroactively shift authority. The migration engine captures `Object.*` / `Array.*` / `JSON.*` /
   719	  `String.*` / `Number.*` at module load for the same reason, and requires `structuredClone` to be available
   720	  at module load or it refuses to construct.
   721	
   722	### Fail-closed contracts (consolidated)
   723	
   724	| Layer | Open the gate when | Fail-closed when | Substitute offered |
   725	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
   726	| Storage | IndexedDB available + quota present | `STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE` / `STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` | Block save; readable error in UI. |
   727	| Case record | Sanitize all-or-nothing pass | Any unbounded value / unsupported type / cycle / prototype drift | Reject save; existing record untouched. |
   728	| Portable bundle | Strict allowlist + value constraints satisfied | Off-allowlist or constraint failure | Field excluded + logged; required-field failure rejects the bundle. |
   729	| Migration (`_sanitize`) | All four walks pass: `_isJsonSafe` (depth ≤ 256), `_isAccessorFreeDescriptorTree` (depth ≤ 256), `_safeJsonStringify` (depth ≤ 256), byte size ≤ `MAX_RECORD_BYTES_DEF` (8 MB or override) | Depth exceeded, non-plain prototype, accessor descriptor, BigInt/symbol/function/non-finite number, cyclic, Proxy input, byte cap exceeded | `RECORD_NOT_AN_OBJECT` / `PROXY_INPUT_REJECTED` / `RECORD_CIRCULAR` / `RECORD_TOO_LARGE`; record stays at prior schema; UI blocks the capability with a reason. |
   730	| Migration envelope | Migrator result is well-shaped + writes commit atomically | Future version, missing path, post-migration invalid, migrator threw, backend rejected, confirm missing, envelope-version mismatch, journal overflow, producer-attestation field | One of the 14 envelope reason codes; envelope `status ∈ { migrated, no-op, rejected, failed }`. |
   731	| C2 lap | All 5 authorities pass | Any single authority blocks | Reason code, no value. |
   732	| C3 distance | Declared lap-distance authority + policy pass | Any of 16 distance reason codes | Reason code, no normalized axis. |
   733	| C4 reference | Explicit user selection present | No selection (no auto fastest_valid / median / best_sector_composite) | `REFERENCE_NOT_SELECTED`; comparison blocked. |
   734	| C4 segmentation / pairing | Corner descriptor present and unambiguous | Degenerate / missing / unresolved pairing | Per-corner block; other corners unaffected. |
   735	| C5 delta | Pairing + normalization + channel availability | Any upstream gate open | Per-metric block. |
   736	| C6 export | Envelope validator passes | Raw / oversized arrays / off-allowlist fields | Envelope refused; no emission. |
   737	| D2 graph | Authoritative inputs verified via WeakSet | Any input not WeakSet-member | Graph build aborts. |
   738	| D3 hypothesis | Verified graph + inputs satisfy hypothesis preconditions | Any precondition fails (e.g. no calibration for a magnitude claim) | Hypothesis downgraded to directional or omitted. |
   739	| D5 brief | Deep-frozen emit; generation token bound to inputs | Token stale / retired / replayed against a different case | Brief refused. |
   740	| R3.0E timeline | Append-only invariants pass (unique `eventId`, non-decreasing `createdAt`, current schema, shape re-validated) | Duplicate / out-of-order / future schema / shape mismatch | Reason code, no append. |
   741	| R3.0E follow-up link | `parentCaseId` matches reverse index AND link; `markParentStatus` in `{present, archived, deleted}` | Reverse-index mismatch / non-array / status off-allowlist | `R3_0E_LINK_CORRUPTED` / `R3_0E_LINK_PARENT_STATUS_INVALID`. |
   742	| Outcome classifier | Same-case/same-session/explicit-reference attestation + control-variable observations | Cross-case, cross-session, no reference, low comparability, or a missing/out-of-range control variable | `invalid_comparison` (highest precedence) or `inconclusive_due_to_confounders` with the specific limitation code. |
   743	| Feature Registry | `featureRegistryActivationAllowed === true` | Otherwise | Feature deferred (navigation hidden / disabled). |
   744	
   745	### What this means for the user
   746	
   747	The product never invents a number to fill a gap. When a metric or recommendation is unavailable, the UI says
   748	**why** in concrete terms ("no explicit reference lap selected", "lap-distance authority missing on this
   749	session", "no road-wheel steering calibration — directional tendency offered instead"). The directional /
   750	qualitative substitute is offered where it is honestly available; otherwise the capability is blocked.
   751	
   752	### Frozen invariants summarized
   753	
   754	- **R3.0B case-record schema** — frozen at v1.4.0, untouched through R3.0F. Every D / E / F phase carries an
   755	  explicit `r3bCaseRecordSchemaUntouched: true` governance assertion.
   756	- **Preset count** — 501. The R2 preset library equivalence guard remains green through every R3 phase.
   757	- **`package.json` version** — `1.4.0`. The version bump to `2.0.0` is staged at F6 (Release Gate). Until F6,
   758	  any version drift fails the train validator closed.
   759	- **`featureRegistryActivationAllowed`** is a per-phase flag, not one train-wide switch. R3.0C, R3.0D, and
   760	  R3.0E have each already flipped their own flag to `true` at their respective `*_ACTIVATION` checkpoints —
   761	  the Comparisons pane is reachable to the user today; the Engineer Brief, Experiment Loop, and Case Timeline
   762	  nav nodes are registered and live in the shipped registry, but their pane content is not yet wired into
   763	  `renderer/index.html` (the D5 Engineer Brief mount ships hidden/inert; the R3.0E viewmodel scripts are not
   764	  loaded by the page).
   765	  Only R3.0F's own flag remains `false` until `F6_RELEASE` (R3.0F introduces no case-scoped pane of its own).
   766	- **`runtimeConsumersAllowed`** — `true` since F1. The migration engine is the first F-phase runtime consumer.
   767	- **Comparison scope** — same-case + same-session only. Cross-case and cross-session are permanently forbidden.
   768	- **Delta sign** — `comparison − reference`. Single convention. No alternate.
   769	- **Reference selection** — explicit user only. `fastest_valid / median_valid / best_sector_composite` are
   770	  permanently disabled.
   771	- **R3.0E append-only is per store, not per phase.** Only `timeline-store` is strictly append-only.
   772	  `experiment-store` exposes `create / update / remove`; `follow-up-link-store` exposes `markParentStatus`;
   773	  `outcome-store` exposes `create / get / listForExperiment` only. The honesty contract is preserved through
   774	  schema-version, stale-write, duplicate-event, out-of-order, and reverse-index guards.
   775	- **No intermediate release, and no branch protection bypass.** No tag, no GitHub release, no semver bump
   776	  between R3.0C and F6; the R3-GATE0 ruleset on `main` (required PR + required `trusted-verification` check +
   777	  no force-push + no deletion, 0 bypass actors) holds across every R3 phase. A force-merge or rule-bypass would
   778	  fail the governance verification step that every phase asserts.
   779	- **R4 is out of scope for this release.** Any capability id whose name matches `^r4_` / `^r40_` / `^r4.0_` /
   780	  `^r4.` is refused at the schema layer — the regex list is an enforcement mechanism, not a teaser. The R3.0
   781	  release does not reference, ship, or pre-wire R4 features.
   782	
   783	### What this architecture is NOT
   784	
   785	- **Not a professional race-engineer replacement.** This is a product-level positioning statement, not a
   786	  Brief `limitations[]` entry — see "What R3.0D refuses to be" above for the structural (causal-overclaim
   787	  scanner) mechanism that enforces the equivalent guarantee.
   788	- **Not a full multi-body-dynamics simulator.** The physics core remains the steady-state single-point model
   789	  R2 established.
   790	- **Not a complete tyre model.** Tyre evidence is consumed where it exists; tyre state is not synthesized.
   791	- **Not a GPS racing-line / lap-optimization tool.** GPS is not promoted to a distance authority.
   792	- **Not a telemetry decoder for arbitrary binary formats.** The supported import paths are the ones R2.3+
   793	  declared; the rest are blocked.
   794	- **Not a measurement device.** Nothing measured is claimed without the data + calibration to back it; nothing
   795	  is fabricated to fill a gap. Synthetic stays synthetic.
   796	
   797	The R3.0 phases compose into a workflow that records what the user has, what the services could conclude from
   798	it, what they refused to conclude and why, what the user tried next, and what happened. That composition is
   799	the entire runtime and topology contract of the R3.0 release; conclusions about what the user *should do next*
   800	remain the user's, informed by the structured evidence the services surface.
     1	# R3 User Workflow — End-to-End
     2	
     3	This document traces a single user's path through the R3 application surface, from the empty-library first launch to exporting a comparison bundle. It is a workflow companion to `docs/credibility-and-trust.md`, `docs/product-positioning.md`, `docs/r2-capability-map.md`, `docs/analysis-workspace-architecture.md`, `docs/r3-experiment-loop.md`, and `docs/r3-credibility-model.md`. It does not repeat the credibility ladder definitions; it shows where each rung is enforced as the user moves through the screens.
     4	
     5	The ladder, in shorthand, is the only basis for what the UI is allowed to render at each step:
     6	
     7	> Physics > Model > Measured > Derived > Heuristic > Unavailable
     8	
     9	Every conclusion described below carries `credibility` and `limitations` at minimum; most also carry `confidence` and `provenance`. The remaining honesty-contract fields (a fail-closed reason, the supporting evidence, what to do to upgrade the claim) exist on most producers but under different field names per producer — see `docs/r3-architecture.md`'s introduction for the exact names per layer. The R3.0E Outcome object specifically has no `credibility`/`confidence`/`provenance`/next-validation field at all (see `docs/r3-experiment-loop.md`). The product never approximates around a missing input; it blocks with a machine-readable reason.
    10	
    11	A note on version numbers used in this document: "the case-record schema frozen at v1.4.0" is a human-readable label for "the on-disk shape of a Case record as it stood when the package version was 1.4.0" — it is **not** a literal version string stored in code. The actual schema-version field is `CASE_SCHEMA_VERSION` in `renderer/js/schema-migration.js`, a plain integer (`1` today), and F1's migrators compare against that integer, not against any `"v1.4.0"` string. The **package application version** is `1.4.0` today, with a target of `2.0.0` post-merge of the integrated delivery train. The two are related but distinct: a future application version bump does not, on its own, advance `CASE_SCHEMA_VERSION`.
    12	
    13	---
    14	
    15	## First launch (empty library)
    16	
    17	On the very first launch the local store is empty. R3.0B has provisioned an IndexedDB-backed `storage-backend.js` with five stores — `cases`, `caseIndex`, `sessions`, `sessionIndex`, `meta` — that `case-store.js` and `session-store.js` build on; in Node test mode the same contracts run against an in-memory backend. There is no remote sync, no account, no cloud — every artefact lives on the user's device.
    18	
    19	The application runs inside an Electron host whose `BrowserWindow` `webPreferences` explicitly set exactly three keys — `preload` (the bridge script path), `contextIsolation: true`, and `nodeIntegration: false` — with no safety-relevant key (`sandbox`, `webSecurity`, etc.) ever explicitly weakened; Electron's own defaults apply to everything not listed. The preload bridge exposes exactly two values to the renderer on `window.electronAPI` — `{platform, version}` — and nothing else. The renderer cannot reach Node, the file system, or arbitrary IPC; every privileged operation goes through the storage backend's typed contract. The R3.0B `backend.transact({ stores, reads, compute })` contract is the single channel for **atomic multi-step reads/writes**; single-key reads also go through `backend.get(ns, key)` and namespace-wide reads through `backend.list(ns)` directly (e.g. `case-store.js`'s `open`/`list`/`compact`) — `transact` is for the atomicity guarantee, not the only persistence entry point. (See `docs/r3-architecture.md` "Electron host boundaries" for the full webPreferences/CSP contract.)
    20	
    21	Two activation flags govern what the runtime is allowed to do, and `featureRegistryActivationAllowed` is tracked **per phase**, not as one train-wide switch. `runtimeConsumersAllowed` has been `true` since F1 — production services bind to authoritative inputs and emit results. R3.0C, R3.0D, and R3.0E have each already flipped their own `featureRegistryActivationAllowed` to `true` at their respective activation checkpoints (`C8_ACTIVATION`, `D5_ENGINEER_BRIEF_ACTIVATION`, `E5_ACTIVATION`) — the Comparisons workspace is live and usable today, and the Engineer Brief / Experiment Loop / Case Timeline **nav nodes** are live in the navigation; their pane content, however, is not yet wired into `renderer/index.html` (the D5 Engineer Brief mount ships hidden/inert, and the page does not load the R3.0E viewmodel scripts — pane wiring is a downstream product step). Only R3.0F's own flag remains `false` until `F6_RELEASE`, and R3.0F introduces no case-scoped pane of its own for that flag to gate. Any path that would activate a capability before its own phase's flag is true still fails closed with a named reason code, regardless of whether the underlying service is otherwise ready.
    22	
    23	The first-launch surface is the Cases section — `shellSection` initialises to `'cases'` (`renderer/index.html`) — whose Case Library + Analysis Workspace pane shows:
    24	
    25	- A clean library state. No fabricated demo cases are listed in the user's library.
    26	- One navigation click away, the Setup Library section hosts the Vehicle Preset Browser (opened from its landing pane's Vehicle Presets card), surfacing the 501 frozen presets. Preset count is a CI/governance invariant enforced by `scripts/check-preset-integrity.js` and `tests/vehicle-preset-pipeline.test.js` — a drift fails the trusted-verification gate before merge. `main.js`'s production startup path has no runtime preset-count check; it does not refuse to start on drift.
    27	- Two calls to action: **Load Demo Analysis Case** (the golden path; `renderer/js/i18n-workspace.js`'s `aw.label.load_demo_analysis_case`) or import telemetry and click **Build Case & Run Analysis** (`aw.label.build_case_run_analysis`) to run an analysis over real data. Note the import path is a **transient** analysis: it persists the telemetry session (`sessionStore.put`) and builds an in-memory analysis view over a demo vehicle/setup binding, but it does **not** create or persist a new case-store Case, and it does not register an authoritative case with the R3.0C C8 authority (`renderer/index.html`'s `runImportedAnalysis` states this explicitly — "no case-store boundary ... until a future product step authorizes import-derived authority"). There is no separate bare "create an empty Case" button.
    28	
    29	The Demo Analysis Case is the only Case that ships pre-populated. Its narrative is produced by production code — the same services that run on a user's real Case — not by hardcoded copy. It exists so a user can see the full capability map exercised end-to-end before they have any telemetry of their own.
    30	
    31	Nothing on the first-launch screen claims engineer-grade sign-off, lap-time gains, or measured handling. Capabilities that require telemetry or calibration are visibly marked blocked, each carrying a machine-readable reason code (`NO_ANALYSIS_CASE`, `NO_STEERING_CHANNEL`, `NO_SPEED_CHANNEL`, `NO_COMPARISON`, etc. — produced by `renderer/js/quantitative-setup-recommendation.js` and mapped to labels in `renderer/js/i18n-shell.js`). The UI renders the code's localized label via `tCode('ui.blocker', code)` (`renderer/index.html`), not the raw code string verbatim; an unknown code falls back to a humanized rendering so a missing translation is never invisible.
    32	
    33	---
    34	
    35	## Create or open a Case
    36	
    37	A Case is the case-centric primary object defined in R3.0A and persisted under the R3 case-record schema frozen at `v1.4.0` in R3.0B. The schema has not been modified through R3.0F and will not be modified until a future major; F1 migration treats `v1.4.0` as the baseline.
    38	
    39	When the user creates a Case they supply:
    40	
    41	- A vehicle preset (one of 501 frozen presets, or a user-edited copy).
    42	- A setup (the active setup snapshot at Case creation; mutations after import are tracked, not silently rewritten).
    43	- Case metadata is auto-derived at save time, not typed in: the case-record schema accepts an optional descriptive label and track identity, but the current UI's only creation paths (`saveCurrentCase` / `saveCurrentCaseAsNew` in `renderer/index.html`) auto-derive the title from the case header (`'Untitled case'` fallback) and store `track: null` — a labelling/track-entry affordance is not yet wired and remains a future product step. The track identity R3.0C uses to refuse cross-track comparison is the explicit `{trackId, layoutId, source:'explicit'}` lap-evidence/association identity, not this metadata field.
    44	
    45	Opening an existing Case rehydrates the workspace through `_r3cBeginCaseTransition`, which is the single transition helper introduced in R3.0C C8. It bumps the session-authority token, clears any prior reference selection, clears the cached comparison authority, and notifies the rest of the shell that a Case has been re-opened. The view model never trusts a caller-provided `eligible` / `confirmed` / `validated` flag — authority is re-derived from raw evidence on every open.
    46	
    47	The Case record carries no measured handling number at this stage. Anything the UI shows is either Physics (closed-form from preset geometry) or Model (a prediction). No measured K_us value, no lap-time figure, no causation language appears.
    48	
    49	---
    50	
    51	## Import telemetry (CSV / canonical)
    52	
    53	Telemetry import is intentionally a separate path from setup. Two import flows are available:
    54	
    55	1. **CSV import** — the universal Telemetry CSV Viewer path. The user picks a CSV; the importer runs preflight diagnostics, surfaces unmapped columns, refuses non-monotonic timebases, and refuses files whose quoting is unclosed. Each diagnostic is a structured `{code, params, message}` entry — codes are language-neutral, the rendered message is localised.
    56	2. **Canonical-format import** — for telemetry already produced by an upstream tool in the project's canonical JSON shape.
    57	
    58	The importer enforces a hard set of fail-closed conditions before the data is admitted into a Case:
    59	
    60	| Condition | Behaviour |
    61	|---|---|
    62	| Unmapped channel | Surfaced; blocks any capability that requires that channel. Mapping is explicit, never inferred. |
    63	| Mapped-but-unconfirmed channel | Blocked. A mapping is not a confirmation. |
    64	| Non-monotonic timebase | Block. The observation services refuse to run. |
    65	| Too-sparse samples | Block with reason code; no resampling-as-rescue. |
    66	| Quoting / structural errors | Fatal at parse — no partial admission. |
    67	| Provenance unknown | Stamped `unverified`. Synthetic stays synthetic; the importer will not relabel synthetic as real. |
    68	
    69	After import the canonical telemetry session carries a `dataProvenance` field of `synthetic`, `real`, or `unverified` (`renderer/js/canonical-telemetry-session.js`). This value is machine-read at the boundary; downstream code never re-infers it from presence.
    70	
    71	The act of importing telemetry does NOT, on its own, unlock the Measured rung. It unlocks eligibility *checks* — each capability is then evaluated against its own gate.
    72	
    73	---
    74	
    75	## Review measured / observable capability tier
    76	
    77	After import, the workspace surfaces the per-capability evaluation. This is the same Capability Map architecture described in `docs/r2-capability-map.md`, now embedded inside the Case-centric shell. Each capability resolves to one of:
    78	
    79	- **Available** — works on model inputs alone; the credibility rung is Physics or Model.
    80	- **Conditionally Available** — gated on confirmed channels + calibration + sample quality; the credibility rung, once gates pass, is Measured or Derived.
    81	- **Blocked** — fail-closed; the UI shows the reason code and offers the honest substitute, never an approximation.
    82	- **Deferred** — planned for a later milestone; the UI says so explicitly.
    83	
    84	The two most-commonly-blocked capabilities at this stage:
    85	
    86	- **Measured K_us** — requires confirmed telemetry **and** a verified road-wheel steering calibration bound to the mapping and session. Without that calibration, the directional tendency observation is offered instead, capped at confidence `medium`. The UI never serialises a K_us magnitude when the calibration is absent. The reasoning for why this number is never named in the directional yaw context — including the F312 yaw-response validation that demonstrated the kinematic-only signal-to-noise gap — is documented in `docs/credibility-and-trust.md` under the F312 yaw-response section.
    87	- **Quantitative setup advice in clicks** — blocked product-wide. There is no validated per-car click-to-rate mapping. Setup-related recommendations downstream are emitted in physical units (Nm/deg, N/mm, %) only. The user will not see a "+2 clicks rear" recommendation anywhere.
    88	
    89	For each capability the UI shows an available/blocked chip summary; blocked reason codes and limitations are surfaced in the Context/Trust panel and the evidence drawer. The full five-column matrix — capability, required input, credibility once eligible, fail-closed conditions, and limitations — lives in `docs/r2-capability-map.md`, not as an in-app table. The user can still audit, before going further, exactly why a number is or is not allowed — via the chips plus the surfaced reason codes.
    90	
    91	The yaw response observation, where eligible, is the only response observation in this version. It separates left- and right-hand turns by raw steering sign, never assumes a symmetric vehicle, and uses the log-gap near-centre guard to keep micro-steer noise out of the ratio. The reported number is a directional yaw gain ratio with confidence; it is never converted to a road-wheel ratio without a user-confirmed steering ratio, and it never names K_us. See `docs/credibility-and-trust.md` for the F312 calibration finding that this restriction encodes.
    92	
    93	---
    94	
    95	## Explicit reference lap selection (same case + same session)
    96	
    97	R3.0C C4 introduced reference selection. The product makes one rule loudly explicit:
    98	
    99	> The reference lap is chosen by the user, explicitly, and only from laps in the **same Case** and the **same session** as the comparison lap.
   100	
   101	The UI does not offer "fastest valid", "median", "best sector composite", or any automatic reference. Those modes do not exist in the product. The reasoning is in `docs/credibility-and-trust.md`: a "best" lap chosen by the tool would silently substitute the tool's judgement for the user's, and would let cross-session or cross-Case comparisons sneak in where tyre, track, and weather context differ.
   102	
   103	Selection flow:
   104	
   105	1. The user picks the comparison lap from the session lap list.
   106	2. The user picks the reference lap from the same session's lap list.
   107	3. The Case-authority + session-authority WeakSets verify the laps belong to the open Case and open session. A `sessionId` string match alone is not enough — the C8 second WeakSet refuses sessionId-string forgery.
   108	4. Corner segmentation (C4) runs over both laps using the Case's track identity (C2) and normalized distance (C3).
   109	5. Corner pairing (C4) produces (reference corner, comparison corner) pairs.
   110	6. Delta metrics (C5) compute the allowlisted six metrics with the fixed delta sign:
   111	
   112	> delta = (comparison − reference)
   113	
   114	Never the reverse. The sign is constant across every metric, every export, every brief.
   115
