   387	- It does not delete comparison bundles the user previously exported — those are files the user wrote to a location they chose. The product does not maintain a registry of past exports.
   388	
   389	### Honesty about deletion
   390	
   391	Deletion is what it claims to be: a removal of the local store entries. It is not a cryptographic wipe of the underlying storage device, and the documentation does not claim it is. A user with disk-level recovery concerns should use OS-level secure-delete tooling on the `userData` folder.
   392	
   393	---
   394	
   395	## R3.0F F2 end-to-end coverage
   396	
   397	The F2 phase ships nine end-to-end flows under `tests/e2e/` — a Node-only logic harness (`tests/e2e/helpers/flow-harness.js`) that composes the production storage, migration, and R3.0E store modules over an in-memory `MemoryBackend()`, not a browser, DOM, Electron process, or IndexedDB. Each flow exercises the storage + migration pipeline for its scenario; not every flow invokes every producer end-to-end (for example flow-06 appends an `outcome_classified` timeline event directly rather than calling the R3.0E classifier). They are listed here so a reader of this document can trace any data-flow claim above to a concrete test:
   398	
   399	| File | Purpose |
   400	|---|---|
   401	| `tests/e2e/flow-01-new-user.test.js` | A fresh `MemoryBackend()` instance (Node-only logic harness, not IndexedDB or a browser) produces a deterministic empty Case Library; F1 reports zero records; envelope is at v1; forbidden actions are disabled. |
   402	| `tests/e2e/flow-02-real-telemetry.test.js` | A CSV-import-style telemetry session lands deterministically into `sessions`; F1 treats the session as at-target; no raw telemetry leaks into the case bundle. |
   403	| `tests/e2e/flow-03-measured.test.js` | A case with measured-metric data preserves the credibility rung exactly `Measured` through storage roundtrip and migration; no credibility upgrade; qualifiers go in `limitations[]`, not the rung. |
   404	| `tests/e2e/flow-04-reference-lap.test.js` | R3.0C reference-lap contract: explicit user selection only (no `fastestValid`, no `medianValid`, no `bestSectorComposite`); comparison authority requires same Case + same Session; delta sign = `comparison − reference`. |
   405	| `tests/e2e/flow-05-vre.test.js` | R3.0D Engineer Brief: authoritative-only inputs; the brief does not classify, does not claim causation, does not blame the driver. |
   406	| `tests/e2e/flow-06-setup-experiment.test.js` | R3.0E Experiment Loop end-to-end: create setup experiment, append an `outcome_classified` timeline event directly (does not call `classifyOutcome`), F1 sees the records as at-target. |
   407	| `tests/e2e/flow-07-driver-experiment.test.js` | Driver-instruction-only experiment plus a follow-up Case-link `create` attempt (does not exercise `listForParent`/`markParentStatus`); follow-up Case Links carry no comparison authority (cross-case forbidden). |
   408	| `tests/e2e/flow-08-export-import.test.js` | Case export produces an R3.0B-validated portable bundle with no raw telemetry; reimport creates an `imported_summary` record, never promoted to `local_full`. |
   409	| `tests/e2e/flow-09-electron-smoke.test.js` | Reads `package.json` to confirm `electron` is declared as a devDependency with a valid semver range; does not invoke the Electron binary or launch a window. |
   410	
   411	## R3.0F F3 hardening probes
   412	
   413	The F3 phase ships six adversarial probes under `tests/e2e/hardening-{01..06}-*.test.js` covering 133 assertions total: the Electron host boundary (preload surface, `webPreferences`, CSP), storage-failure handling (`case-store.remove` confirm-guard, atomic `backend.transact` failure, oversized-record rejection), no-stale-UI invariants (case-id-bearing viewmodel fields after a Case/Session transition), large-library bounded-linear scaling (operation counts at `N` and `2N`), XSS surfaces (no unsafe DOM-injection patterns), and supply-chain integrity (dependency/script declaration allowlist, no committed secrets). The F3 manifest passes at the milestone baseline; the assertions are the canonical machine-readable counterpart to the prose in this document.
   414	
   415	---
   416	
   417	## Summary of guarantees
   418	
   419	- All persistence is local. No cloud, no account, no remote ID.
   420	- Raw telemetry is never in a case bundle. Only the structured `imported_summary` is.
   421	- `imported_summary` is never promoted to `Measured` automatically. Promotion requires confirmed mapping + verified calibration + sample-quality gate + re-derived authority. When promotion is not warranted, a `Derived` or `Heuristic` substitute is offered explicitly, or the capability is `Unavailable`.
   422	- Comparison is same-case + same-session, reference-lap explicit-user-only, delta sign `(comparison − reference)`. Follow-up Case Links never carry comparison authority.
   423	- No production network egress. Enforced by CSP `default-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval'` (no `connect-src` declared, so it falls back to `default-src 'self'`) + absence of remote fetch call sites in production paths + R3.0F F3 supply-chain probe verification.
   424	- Electron host explicitly sets `contextIsolation: true` and `nodeIntegration: false`; `sandbox`/`webSecurity`/`allowRunningInsecureContent`/etc. are absent (Electron defaults apply, never explicitly weakened) and the CSP allows `'unsafe-inline'`/`'unsafe-eval'` (not a strict policy). Preload surface is exactly `{ platform, version }` on `window.electronAPI`. R3.0F F3's electron-boundary probe asserts these properties; the XSS probe covers the adjacent DOM-injection attack surface and the supply-chain probe covers the adjacent dependency-surface attack vector — see "Electron host security" above for the exact contract.
   425	- User control is local and immediate. `caseStore.remove(caseId, { confirm: true })` deletes the case row and rewrites `caseIndex.__index` atomically; it does **not** cascade. Sessions and R3.0E records (experiment/outcome/timeline/follow-up-link) require separate removal calls; outcomes and timelines have no `remove()` (write-once / append-only by contract). The R3.0D Engineer Brief needs no removal call — it is never persisted, held only in module-scoped variables that the orchestrator clears on every transition. The R3.0F migration journal and state live as two named keys inside the `meta` store and are wiped only by deleting `userData`.
   426	- R3.0F F1 is deterministic, idempotent, fail-closed, structured-clone-only at the boundary, and **refuses to write producer-attestation field names** with `PRODUCER_ATTESTATION_REFUSED`. There is no `migrations` object store and no `migrationId`; the journal at `meta['__r3_0f_migration_journal__']` (capped at 256 entries by default) and the state at `meta['__r3_0f_migration_state__']` are the audit surface.
   427	- All of the above held across R3.0F F1 migration, F2 nine E2E flows, and F3 six hardening probes (133 assertions) at the milestone baseline.
   428	
   429	The same fail-closed posture that governs the credibility ladder governs the data layer: when a guarantee cannot be honoured on a given run, the operation is blocked with a reason, never approximated, never silently downgraded.
     1	# R3.0 Credibility Model
     2	
     3	This document is the R3.0-specific extension of [`docs/credibility-and-trust.md`](./credibility-and-trust.md). It does not replace the ladder defined there; it pins down the **closed-rung enums** that each R3.0 producer must emit, the **conditions under which each rung may be granted**, what each rung **guarantees and explicitly does NOT guarantee**, and the governance rules — producer attestation, fail-closed defaults, cross-case + cross-session forbidden propagation — that prevent a rung from being upgraded by anything other than the producer that originally derived it.
     4	
     5	The credibility ladder defined in `credibility-and-trust.md` (Physics > Model > Measured > Derived > Heuristic > Unavailable) is the higher-level taxonomy and remains the user-facing labelling throughout R3.0. The contracts that R3.0 producers consume are **closed enums defined in code**: the R3.0C comparison-authority contract (`contracts/r3.0c/credibility-contract.js`) and the R3.0D evidence/conclusion contracts (`contracts/r3.0d/credibility-contract.js`). Both contract modules carry the same opening statement in their headers: *credibility is OWNED by the domain/service (it is an INPUT here); a UI/consumer never derives it. This module only VALIDATES caller-supplied authority — it computes no new measurement and reads no telemetry.*
     6	
     7	Nothing in this document permits a downstream consumer to **upgrade** a rung. A rung may only be **demoted** to `Unavailable` (blocked, with a reason) when a quality gate fails. Upgrade is structurally impossible by the producer-attestation contract: the producer that derived the value is the only authority for its rung, and that producer's attestation is verified, not trusted.
     8	
     9	---
    10	
    11	## The contract enums: three distinct closed enums, defined in code
    12	
    13	There is no single universal three-rung enum in R3.0. There are **three distinct closed enums**, each defined in a contract module and consumed by a specific producer family. A pipeline boundary that crosses between two of them re-validates against the receiving contract; values are never silently re-tagged.
    14	
    15	### R3.0C `CREDIBILITY_LADDER` — comparison-authority credibility (6 values)
    16	
    17	Defined in `contracts/r3.0c/credibility-contract.js` (UMD global `R3_0C_CredibilityContract`). The header comment reads verbatim: *credibility ladder (docs/credibility-and-trust.md) + provenance (R3.0B case-record enums). Frozen.*
    18	
    19	```
    20	CREDIBILITY_LADDER = ["Physics", "Model", "Measured", "Derived", "Heuristic", "Unavailable"]
    21	```
    22	
    23	This is the same six-rung ladder that `credibility-and-trust.md` exposes as the user-facing taxonomy. R3.0C carries it across the comparison-authority boundary as a frozen closed enum. The contract's `REQUIRED_FIELDS` are `["credibility", "provenance", "confidence", "limitations", "blockedReasons"]`; any missing, blank, or out-of-enum field yields the reason code `INSUFFICIENT_CREDIBILITY_METADATA` and the validator returns `{ valid: false, reasonCodes: [...] }`. The companion `PROVENANCE` enum is `["synthetic", "real", "unverified"]` and the companion `CONFIDENCE` enum is `["low", "medium", "high"]`. The R3.0C normalizer (`normalizeCredibilityMetadata`) is a **pass-through assembler**: it validates and then returns a frozen copy of caller-supplied authority, or a blocked result; it derives nothing. `derivedHere: false` on every successful return.
    24	
    25	### R3.0D `EVIDENCE_CREDIBILITY` — evidence-credibility ladder (4 values)
    26	
    27	Defined in `contracts/r3.0d/credibility-contract.js` (UMD global `R3_0D_CredibilityContract`).
    28	
    29	```
    30	EVIDENCE_CREDIBILITY = ["measured", "derived", "heuristic", "synthetic"]
    31	```
    32	
    33	The contract's own header comment reads verbatim: *R3.0D credibility ladder. Directive §8 mandates `measured / derived / heuristic / synthetic` for EVIDENCE credibility (i.e., where the data came from). The five-step ladder from R3.0C is the CONCLUSION credibility (the strength of a downstream claim). D1 keeps both vocabularies cleanly separated so a heuristic source cannot be re-labelled as a measured conclusion.*
    34	
    35	Two practical consequences of the two-vocabulary split:
    36	
    37	1. In the R3.0D evidence layer, `'synthetic'` is a value in **two separate enums on the same EvidenceNode**: the 4-value `EVIDENCE_CREDIBILITY` rung (`measured`/`derived`/`heuristic`/`synthetic`) and the 3-value `PROVENANCE` field (`synthetic`/`real`/`unverified`) — they coexist as two distinct keys (`credibility`, `provenance`) on the node, not one flag wearing two names. The synthetic-honesty enforcement constraint is keyed specifically on **`provenance === 'synthetic'`**, not on `credibility`: `contracts/r3.0d/evidence-node-contract.js` checks `if (n.provenance === 'synthetic') { ... require LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY ... }`. An evidence node sourced from the Demo Analysis Case is tagged `provenance: "synthetic"`, and that triggers the requirement to carry the `LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY` marker among its limitations.
    38	2. In R3.0C and at the R3.0D *conclusion* layer (see below), **`synthetic` is a provenance value**, not a rung. The same word names different things at different layers — and the validators at each boundary enforce the layer-correct enum.
    39	
    40	### R3.0D `CONCLUSION_CREDIBILITY` — conclusion-credibility ladder (6 values)
    41	
    42	Defined alongside `EVIDENCE_CREDIBILITY` in `contracts/r3.0d/credibility-contract.js`.
    43	
    44	```
    45	CONCLUSION_CREDIBILITY = ["Physics", "Model", "Measured", "Derived", "Heuristic", "Unavailable"]
    46	```
    47	
    48	Identical in members and ordering to R3.0C's `CREDIBILITY_LADDER`. The R3.0D contract uses it for the *strength of a downstream claim* — a hypothesis (`contracts/r3.0d/hypothesis-contract.js`) and the Engineer Brief (`contracts/r3.0d/engineer-brief-contract.js`) both carry a `credibility` field from this enum — separately from the evidence-layer ladder that names *where the data came from*. The R3.0E outcome class is **not** one of these: the Outcome object (`contracts/r3.0e/outcome-contract.js`) has no `credibility` field at all; the "Heuristic" rung assigned to Outcome Classification elsewhere in this document is a documentation-level classification of the producer stage, not a literal field the classifier emits (see `docs/r3-experiment-loop.md`). The split is what the D1 contract calls "cleanly separated so a heuristic source cannot be re-labelled as a measured conclusion."
    49	
    50	### Confidence at D1: closed-state enum only, no numeric value
    51	
    52	R3.0D's confidence at the **contract layer (D1)** is unusually strict: `CONFIDENCE_STATES` is the closed enum `["unresolved", "not_computed"]`, with the contract comment verbatim: *Confidence state at D1 — closed enum. There is NO numeric `value` field allowed at D1.* The discipline comment elaborates: *A caller CANNOT directly supply a numeric confidence at D1. Confidence is either: (a) UNRESOLVED — explicit `{ state: 'unresolved' }` marker, OR (b) NOT_COMPUTED — explicit `{ state: 'not_computed' }` marker.* `validateConfidenceShape(c)` therefore enforces a closed-key plain object whose only allowed own key is `state`; numeric `value`, `score`, `numeric`, or `probability` keys at the **D1 caller layer** are rejected.
    53	
    54	The numeric confidence is computed **inside the R3.0D engines** — not at the D1 caller layer — under a strict D1 → D3 → D4 split:
    55	
    56	| Layer | Producer | What it emits / accepts |
    57	| --- | --- | --- |
    58	| D1 | Caller-supplied input | Closed-state only: `{ state: 'unresolved' }` or `{ state: 'not_computed' }`. Numeric fields rejected. |
    59	| **D3 — HypothesisEngine** | `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js` | Computes the numeric `confidenceScore` deterministically and emits `{ state, score }` on each Hypothesis it produces. This is the **first authoritative numeric confidence value** in the R3.0D pipeline. |
    60	| **D4 — PriorityEngine** | `renderer/js/r3-0d-priority-engine.js` | Validates and consumes the D3 export (`verifyAuthoritativeHypothesisSet`); applies its prioritisation rules; emits a PrioritySet that references D3's confidences. D4 does not produce a new numeric confidence; it consumes D3's. |
    61	
    62	The D1 contract module's own header comment that names "D4_PRIORITY_ENGINE" as the numeric producer is stale relative to the shipped D3/D4 split — D3 is where the numeric `confidenceScore` is actually computed. Any cap a downstream engine applies (e.g., a heuristic-source cap) lives in that engine's module, not in this contract layer.
    63	
    64	### Availability at D1: closed evidence-graph enum
    65	
    66	```
    67	AVAILABILITY = ["available", "unavailable", "partial", "unconfirmed"]
    68	```
    69	
    70	Contract comment verbatim: *Availability enum — used by the evidence graph (whether a metric / channel is currently usable).* The validator (`validateAvailability`) rejects out-of-enum values with `EVIDENCE_OBSERVATION_INVALID`.
    71	
    72	### Why the enums are closed
    73	
    74	Each enum is **closed**: a producer whose output cannot fit one of the listed values **must emit `Unavailable`** at the conclusion layer (with a fail-closed reason) or fail its evidence validator (with an `EVIDENCE_*_INVALID` reason code). There is no "estimated", no "approximate", no "best-effort" path. The R3.0C and R3.0D validators are both `buildBlockedResult`-shaped: a failing input does not return a degraded value, it returns `{ valid: false, reasonCodes: [...] }` and the consumer fails closed.
    75	
    76	---
    77	
    78	## How values move between the enums
    79	
    80	A value's rung at the evidence layer (`EVIDENCE_CREDIBILITY`) and its rung at the conclusion layer (`CONCLUSION_CREDIBILITY`) are **separately attested by separately validated emissions**. There is no implicit promotion path between them. The decision engine is what binds an evidence-layer rung to a conclusion-layer rung, and it does so under the authoritative-only-inputs rule (R3.0D's closure-private WeakSet verification): conclusions are admitted only when their evidence inputs were verified to originate from the live evidence-graph producer in the current run.
    81	
    82	Two patterns this enables:
    83	
    84	- **Evidence `measured` → Conclusion `Measured`** is the canonical "honest pass-through" path: the evidence-graph producer attested a `measured` evidence node on confirmed, calibrated channels in a same-case + same-session window; the conclusion producer consumed it through the WeakSet verifier and emitted a `Measured` conclusion. The synthetic-honesty constraint did not fire because the evidence rung was not `synthetic`.
    85	- **Evidence `provenance: "synthetic"` → Conclusion at any rung, but with `LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY`** is the Demo Analysis Case path: an evidence node carries `provenance: "synthetic"` plus the `LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY` marker; any conclusion built on it carries the limitation forward and may never be presented as a real-world claim. The contract validators at both layers refuse to forget the synthetic origin.
    86	
    87	A value emitted on a prior run, persisted, and rehydrated on a later run does **not** retain runtime authority. The R3.0E stores module header makes this explicit, verbatim: *Persisted records carry NO runtime authority (WeakSet identity is closure-private and cannot survive reload). Rehydration consumers MUST re-validate via the E1 contracts before treating values as authoritative.*
    88	
    89	---
    90	
    91	## R3.0C comparison authority: same-case + same-session, explicit reference only
    92	
    93	R3.0C's comparison-authority contract carries the full six-rung `CREDIBILITY_LADDER` and validates caller-supplied credibility metadata in a single direction: it accepts a producer's emission and returns a frozen pass-through, or it returns a blocked result. The contract has no producer of its own — it is the authority gate between the comparison-authority pipeline and downstream consumers.
    94	
    95	The comparison authority itself is structurally constrained by three rules that the contract validator presupposes:
    96	
    97	1. **Same-case + same-session only.** Comparison and reference laps must both belong to the same Analysis Case and the same session of that case. Both checks are enforced by R3.0C's reference-selection module (`renderer/js/r3-0c-reference-selection.js`), which rejects a caseId mismatch with `CROSS_CASE_COMPARISON_UNSUPPORTED` and a sessionId mismatch with `CROSS_SESSION_COMPARISON_UNSUPPORTED` — `case-store` itself (R3.0B CRUD/import/export) has no comparison gate of its own.
    98	2. **Reference lap = explicit user selection.** There is no `fastest_valid` auto-pick, no `median` auto-pick, no `best_sector_composite` auto-pick. The user names the reference lap by its identity in the current session.
    99	3. **Delta = comparison − reference** with a fixed sign convention. The delta metric set is allowlisted (the six allowlisted delta metrics live in the R3.0C delta-metrics module). Segmentation is deterministic over the normalized-distance grid. Pairing is deterministic over the corner-identity assignment. Every emission carries `derivedHere: false` because the contract is a pass-through, but the upstream delta producer carries `Derived` on the rung field and `real` (or `synthetic`, preserved) on the provenance field.
   100	
   101	When any of those preconditions fails, the comparison-authority pipeline emits a blocked envelope built by `buildBlockedResult` (`contracts/r3.0c/reason-codes.js` lines 169-180) via the reference-selection module's `_blocked` wrapper (`renderer/js/r3-0c-reference-selection.js`): `{ eligible: false, status: 'blocked', reasonCodes, explanationKeys, detail, selection: null, result: null }`, carrying one of the 55+ R3.0C reason codes. This shape is distinct from `REQUIRED_FIELDS` (`credibility-contract.js` line 27) — that array governs what a caller-supplied credibility-metadata object must contain before `validateCredibilityMetadata` will accept it as valid input, and is never checked against this blocked envelope. The fail-closed guarantee here is structural, not a REQUIRED_FIELDS check: `buildBlockedResult` always sets `result: null` (no numeric/comparison payload ever rides on a blocked result) and normalizes `reasonCodes` to never be empty, so the consumer fails closed.
   102	
   103	---
   104	
   105	## R3.0D Engineer Brief: authoritative-only inputs, no LLM, no causation, no driver blame
   106	
   107	R3.0D's Engineer Brief is the user-facing surface of the decision engine. `buildEngineerBrief` (`renderer/js/r3-0d-engineer-brief.js`) takes exactly two authoritative-only inputs: `{ hypothesisSet, prioritySet }`, each verified through the closure-private WeakSet pattern. (The applied-change record and controlled-variables record belong to a different stage — the R3.0E E3 outcome classifier — not to D5.) The brief is **deterministic** and contains **no runtime LLM call**: there is no model invocation, no external network call, no probabilistic sampling. Every sentence the brief surfaces is produced by a closed-form composition of the hypothesis/priority inputs through R3.0D's emit modules.
   108	
   109	Three substantive constraints the brief honours on every emission:
   110	
   111	- **No causation.** A correlation in the evidence graph is not promoted to a causal claim. The hypothesis engine surfaces directional and consistency observations; it does not assert "X caused Y." This is enforced structurally, not by a standing "no-causation" limitation marker: `contracts/r3.0d/engineer-brief-contract.js` and `recommendation-contract.js` scan every i18n-key string for causal-overclaim wording and reject the whole record with `HYPOTHESIS_CAUSAL_OVERCLAIM` if any is found — there is no `LIMITATION_NO_CAUSAL_CLAIM` code anywhere in the codebase.
   112	- **No driver blame.** Driver-input observations are reported as driver observations and never re-framed as coaching directives. The Engineer Brief is a vehicle-and-setup surface; a coaching directive requires an entirely separate authority chain (which R3.0F does not enable).
   113	- **Synthetic stays synthetic.** Evidence nodes with `provenance: "synthetic"` arrive carrying `LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY` and the brief preserves the marker on every conclusion it builds from them. There is no "demo mode" that strips synthetic limitations.
   114	
   115	The Engineer Brief also obeys the `limitations[]` propagation rule: `renderer/js/r3-0d-engineer-brief.js` builds `limitations` as the deduplicated union of `hypothesisSet.limitations` and every individual hypothesis's `limitations` — no brief-level limitation is added on top of that union, and there is no standing no-causation marker added to it (no-causation is enforced structurally at the contract layer, as described above, not via a limitation code).
   116	
   117	---
   118	
   119	## R3.0E stores: only the timeline is contractually append-only
   120	
   121	The R3.0E stores live in `renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js` (UMD global `R3_0E_Stores`). The module header lists four persistence semantics, verbatim:
   122	
   123	- *Every payload validated by its E1 contract BEFORE write (in `compute()`).*
   124	- *Every payload re-validated on read; future-schemaVersion → fail-closed reject.*
   125	- *Timeline events append-only; duplicate eventId rejected; out-of-order timestamp rejected.*
   126	- *Persisted records carry NO runtime authority (WeakSet identity is closure-private and cannot survive reload). Rehydration consumers MUST re-validate via the E1 contracts before treating values as authoritative.*
   127	
   128	The second bullet is accurate for single-record reads (`get()`; the per-link reads inside `listForParent()`), but has one runtime exception the header comment doesn't call out: `experimentStore.list()` and `outcomeStore.listForExperiment()` (`renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js:149`, `:213`) read directly from the lightweight index stores and return those values as-is, with no schema-version or shape check.
   129	
   130	The four producer stores are distinct in their mutation discipline. Earlier drafts of this document spoke of "experiment, outcome, follow-up, and timeline stores all append-only with monotonic createdAt; no entry is mutated or reordered after emission." That is not what the code does. The accurate picture:
   131	
   132	### Timeline store — true append-only (the only store with that contract)
   133	
   134	`createTimelineStore(backend)` exposes `getTimeline(caseId)` and `appendEvent(caseId, event)`. The append path uses the non-mutating pattern `var nextEvents = existing.events.concat([event])` and enforces, on every append, the reason codes:
   135	
   136	- `R3_0E_TIMELINE_FUTURE_SCHEMA` on schemaVersion overflow,
   137	- `R3_0E_TIMELINE_DUPLICATE_EVENT` if `eventId` already exists in `events[]`,
   138	- `R3_0E_TIMELINE_OUT_OF_ORDER` if a new `createdAt` is earlier than the previous event's `createdAt` (non-decreasing; equal timestamps are accepted by the store — the E4 service below is stricter and does reject equality),
   139	- `R3_0E_TIMELINE_INVALID` on contract validation failure.
   140	
   141	The E4 service `renderer/js/r3-0e-followup-timeline.js` makes the contract even stricter for the production entry points (`createFollowUpLink`, `appendTimelineEvent`, `projectTimeline`, `listFollowUpLinksForParent`). Its header reads verbatim:
   142	
   143	- *Append-only timeline. Existing events are NEVER overwritten. Corrections are expressed as NEW events whose params reference the prior eventId.*
   144	- *Timeline event eventIds are deterministic: hash(caseId, sequence, kind, i18nKey).*
   145	- *Timeline strictly monotonic by createdAt; duplicate eventId rejected; clock rollback yielding a timestamp earlier than the previous event → reject.*
   146	- *Every link / event / projection is deep-frozen and registered in a closure-private WeakSet. The exported verifiers check identity + structural witnesses.*
   147	- *Clock invoked AT MOST ONCE per call, AFTER authority and contract gates. Forged input → zero clock invocations.*
   148	
   149	### Experiment store — not append-only; supports `update()` and `remove()`
   150	
   151	`createExperimentStore(backend)` exposes `create(rec)`, `update(rec)`, `get(experimentId)`, `list()`, and `remove(experimentId)`. Existing experiment definitions can be revised in place and deleted entirely. The store does enforce identity discipline and stale-write protection:
   152	
   153	- `create()` rejects `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_ID_COLLISION` if the key already exists, and rejects records whose `schemaVersion` does not match the contract.
   154	- `update()` rejects `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_MISSING` (no prior record), `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_FUTURE_SCHEMA` (schemaVersion overflow), and `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_STALE_WRITE` (the caller's `createdAt` does not match the persisted record). On success it writes a fresh `updatedAt` from the clock.
   155	- `remove()` deletes both the `EXPERIMENTS` and `EXPERIMENTS_INDEX` entries.
   156	
   157	The mutation discipline is therefore "identity-stable, schema-checked, stale-write-protected" — not append-only.
   158	
   159	### Outcome store — no exposed mutator, but no contractual append-only assertion either
   160	
   161	`createOutcomeStore(backend)` exposes `create(rec)`, `get(outcomeId)`, and `listForExperiment(experimentId)`. There is no `update` and no `remove`. The store is effectively append-only by the absence of mutating verbs, but the code does not declare append-only as a contract (no equivalent of the timeline's "append-only" header line), so the discipline is structural rather than asserted. `create()` rejects `R3_0E_OUTCOME_ID_COLLISION` on duplicate `outcomeId`.
   162	
   163	### Follow-up link store — mutable via `markParentStatus()`
   164	
   165	`createFollowUpLinkStore(backend)` exposes `create(link)`, `get(linkId)`, `listForParent(parentCaseId)`, and `markParentStatus(linkId, newStatus)`. The `markParentStatus()` method **mutates `parentStatus` on existing records** — it reads the current record, builds `Object.assign({}, cur, { parentStatus: newStatus })`, and writes the result back to the same key. Valid statuses are `['present', 'archived', 'deleted']`. `create()` rejects `R3_0E_LINK_ID_COLLISION` on duplicate `linkId` and updates the reverse index `r3_0e_followupLinksByCase` keyed by `parentCaseId`. `listForParent()` additionally closes two earlier Codex findings: (E2-R1-02) every fetched link is re-validated against its contract before being returned; (E2-R2-01) each validated link's `parentCaseId` must match the caller-supplied `parentCaseId`, otherwise the entry is rejected as corruption.
   166	
   167	### Why the distinction matters
   168	
   169	The append-only timeline is the audit substrate: every event's provenance is fixed at emit time, and a later observation of the same physical phenomenon produces an additional event with its own envelope rather than rewriting the prior one. Experiment definitions, by contrast, are editable artefacts in the user's working set — they are not history. The outcome store sits between the two: structurally history-shaped, but without the explicit contract gate the timeline carries. The follow-up link store's `markParentStatus` exists so a follow-up case's parent can be marked archived or deleted without rewriting the link itself; the link record's identity and `parentCaseId` are stable, the lifecycle marker is not.
   170	
   171	Persistence-layer credibility discipline is independent of the store's mutation discipline. Every payload reached through a single-record read (`get()`; the per-link reads inside `listForParent()`) is validated by its E1 contract on write **and** on read; future-schemaVersion records fail closed on read; rehydrated values must be re-validated by the E1 contract before any consumer treats them as authoritative. **Exception:** `experimentStore.list()` and `outcomeStore.listForExperiment()` (`renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js:149`, `:213`) read directly from the lightweight index stores and return those values as-is, with no schema-version or shape check — see `docs/r3-data-and-privacy.md`'s "Persistence semantics" section for the full exception. The closure-private WeakSet identity that the runtime uses to recognise an authoritative emission does not survive reload, by design.
   172	
   173	The R3.0E outcome classifier obeys the authoritative-only-inputs rule inherited from R3.0D: outcomes are classified from a closed five-key input wrapper — the Experiment record, the applied-change audit envelope, the follow-up's same-case/same-session/explicit-reference attestation, the observation (direction/magnitude/evidence ids), and the observed control variables — never from a live evidence-graph read or a caller-supplied outcome label. There is no separate credibility-floor gate in this classifier. An `invalid_comparison` outcome is emitted whenever the follow-up's attestation is not legitimate (cross-case, cross-session, no explicit reference, or low comparability), taking precedence over every other class. A `confirmed` outcome requires same-case, same-session, explicit reference, every declared control variable observed and in range, and the observed direction/magnitude to match the experiment's prediction — and even then it is `confirmed for this experiment's measurement window`, not a generalised vehicle claim. See `docs/r3-experiment-loop.md` "Outcome classes" for the full six-class enum and precedence order.
   174	
   175	---
   176	
   177	## R3.0F F1 migration: never fabricates attestation
   178	
   179	R3.0F F1 is the schema-migration engine. It migrates each persisted store's records toward that store's integer `targetVersion` (currently `1` for every R3.0B/E store; per-store migrator steps live under `scripts/migrators/`). F1 emits **no credibility rung of its own**: it is not a producer that classifies anything on the ladder. The transform is **deterministic by mandate** — given the same input record byte-for-byte, the migrator's steps produce the same output record byte-for-byte. The migrator steps consume no clock, no random source, and no environment; the surrounding engine uses an injected stamp/clock only to write its own journal and state metadata records (not the migrated payloads). The underlying credibility envelope of each migrated record is preserved verbatim — the rung, provenance, limitations[], blockedReasons, and evidence references travel through unchanged.
   180	
   181	The migration engine's central rule on credibility is that it **never fabricates attestation**. A v1.3 input record that lacks producer attestation fields does not gain them across the migration: the migrator does not invent a `producerId`, a `derivedHere` flag, a calibration binding, or a synthetic-vs-real provenance. Worse, an input record that arrives **carrying** attestation sentinel fields the migrator does not consider trustworthy is refused outright with the reason code `PRODUCER_ATTESTATION_REFUSED`. The migrator does not "best-effort coerce" attested-looking fields into a valid attestation shape; it refuses the record and surfaces the reason.
   182	
   183	Two practical consequences:
   184	
   185	1. The synthetic flag (in whichever field carries it) is preserved across the v1.3 → v1.4.0 transform. A `provenance: "synthetic"` value at R3.0C, or an R3.0D evidence node's `provenance: "synthetic"` field (the field the synthetic-honesty constraint is actually keyed on — see above), retains its tag and its `LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY` marker after migration.
   186	2. The `limitations[]` field is preserved verbatim. The migrator does not collapse, dedupe, or rewrite limitation codes — it carries them across in their stable-code form.
   187	
   188	---
   189	
   190	## R3.0F F2 end-to-end flows: nine production flows under `tests/e2e/`
   191	
   192	R3.0F F2 is the end-to-end verification surface. Nine flows live under `tests/e2e/flow-{01..09}-*.test.js`. The flows are **heterogeneous** — each exercises a specific production path rather than all running the same R3.0B→C→D→E traversal on the same fixture. Concretely:
   193	
   194	| Flow | Surface exercised |
   195	| --- | --- |
   196	| `flow-01-new-user.test.js` | Empty-state baseline on a fresh `MemoryBackend`; F1 idempotent skip on an empty backend. |
   197	| `flow-02-real-telemetry.test.js` | Session-store roundtrip; raw telemetry never lands in the case bundle. |
   198	| `flow-03-measured.test.js` | Credibility rung exact `measured`; limitations[] preserved end-to-end; closed-enum membership. |
   199	| `flow-04-reference-lap.test.js` | R3.0C `selectReference` negative paths (forbidden auto modes, missing selection, forged user-selection, stale candidate lap). |
   200	| `flow-05-vre.test.js` | R3.0D `buildEngineerBrief` authority gate; forged hypothesisSet rejected with `HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED`. |
   201	| `flow-06-setup-experiment.test.js` | R3.0E experiment create + timeline append; out-of-order timeline rejected. |
   202	| `flow-07-driver-experiment.test.js` | Driver instruction + follow-up Case Link; cross-case + cross-session comparison forbidden; `assertNoStaleCaseRef` invoked. |
   203	| `flow-08-export-import.test.js` | Bundle export with no raw telemetry; `imported_summary` never promoted; F1 sees imported records as at-target; `assertNoStaleCaseRef` invoked. |
   204	| `flow-09-electron-smoke.test.js` | Static Electron smoke — `package.json` devDeps declaration, `main.js` contextIsolation/nodeIntegration, `preload.js` contextBridge surface. |
   205	
   206	What is asserted on the **flows that actually surface a producer emission with the honesty-contract envelope** (flows 03/04/05/08 — rung, provenance, limitations preserved through storage/migration/export) is that envelope on each emission. Flows 06 and 07 exercise the R3.0E experiment/follow-up-link surface (experiment creation, a directly-appended `outcome_classified` timeline event, a follow-up-link `create` attempt, and stale-case-reference checks) but do not themselves assert a producer's rung/provenance/limitations/blockers/evidenceRefs envelope — see `docs/r3-data-and-privacy.md`'s flow table for what each of the nine flows actually covers. The synthetic marker rides through anywhere a Demo Analysis Case input was used; F1 sees that input verbatim and never promotes it. Same-case + same-session is asserted by attempting forbidden cross-boundaries and being rejected by the R3.0C contract.
   207	
   208	---
   209	
   210	## R3.0F F3 hardening probes: six probes, 133 assertions
   211	
   212	R3.0F F3 is the hardening surface. Six probes live under `tests/e2e/hardening-{01..06}-*.test.js`, totalling 133 assertions:
   213	
   214	- **Electron boundary.** `contextIsolation`/`nodeIntegration` are explicitly set and never weakened; preload exposes only the minimal `contextBridge` surface; the renderer CSP is never weakened.
   215	- **Storage failure.** `case-store.remove` requires explicit `confirm:true`; an atomic `backend.transact` failure leaves the source record completely unchanged (no partial write); an oversized record is rejected by the record-bytes cap.
   216	- **No-stale-UI.** No viewmodel retains a stale case-id reference (across the full documented set of case-id-bearing fields, including R3.0C/D/E `caseAssociation` fields) after a Case/Session transition, verified against a real R3.0E experiment record's production shape.
   217	- **Large library.** The case-store + F1 migration engine scale bounded-linear, not quadratic, as library size grows.
   218	- **XSS.** The renderer never pipes user-supplied or case-derived text into `innerHTML` or `document.write`.
   219	- **Supply-chain.** `package.json` declares only the known Electron/electron-builder dependencies; no production renderer module pulls a bare third-party `require()`; no untrusted script references; no committed secrets/`.env` files.
   220	
   221	These six probes do not exercise the closure-private WeakSet producer-attestation pattern, the structured-clone-only firewall, or `PRODUCER_ATTESTATION_REFUSED` on the migration path — those defences are real (see "Migration boundary" and the R3.0D/E sections of `docs/r3-experiment-loop.md`), but they are verified by their own phase's tests (e.g. `tests/r3.0e-outcome-classifier.test.js`'s WeakSet producer-attestation checks, `tests/r3.0f-migration-engine.test.js`'s `PRODUCER_ATTESTATION_REFUSED` checks), not by the F3 hardening-`{01..06}` files.
   222	
   223	---
   224	
   225	## Cross-case + cross-session forbidden propagation
   226	
   227	R3.0 forbids three specific propagations of credibility-tagged values, all of which would, in different ways, launder a value's origin and break the honesty contract. The forbidden propagations are enforced by the producers themselves, not by convention.
   228	
   229	**1. Cross-case propagation is forbidden.** An R3.0 Analysis Case is the unit of evidence boundary. A value emitted in Case A's session may not be consumed as evidence by Case B's producers, regardless of how similar the cases look on the surface. R3.0E's experiment loop is the most prominent enforcer: a follow-up case may *reference* a prior case as context, but its outcome classifier, timeline, and experiment store never aggregate values from a different case into authoritative inputs. The decision engine's evidence-graph (R3.0D) is keyed on the case identity; an evidence node from a different case identity fails the closure-private verification and is rejected.
   230	
   231	**2. Cross-session propagation within a case is forbidden for comparison and decision authority.** R3.0C comparison is **same-case + same-session only**. A reference lap from a prior session — even from the same case, even from the same track, even from the same vehicle setup — is **not** an admissible reference. The reference lap must be **explicitly selected by the user** from the current session's laps; there is no fastest_valid auto-pick, no median auto-pick, no best_sector_composite auto-pick. R3.0D's hypothesis engine respects the same boundary: hypotheses are formed on evidence drawn from the current session's authoritative graph. Persistent stores (R3.0B) preserve cross-session history for the user to read; they do not feed it back into producer authority.
   232	
   233	**3. Cross-rung promotion is forbidden.** A transform may consume an input and emit a new value at the input's rung or lower; it may never emit at a higher rung. At the R3.0D evidence layer the rule is enforced by the closed `EVIDENCE_CREDIBILITY` enum and the synthetic-honesty constraint (a `synthetic` evidence node never loses its `LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY` marker). At the R3.0D conclusion layer the rule is enforced by the closed `CONCLUSION_CREDIBILITY` enum and the WeakSet identity check (a conclusion is admitted only when its evidence inputs were verified to originate from the live evidence-graph producer in the current run). At the R3.0C comparison layer the rule is enforced by the pass-through normalizer (which derives nothing and refuses to upgrade what the caller supplied).
   234	
   235	The combined effect of the three forbidden propagations is that **the credibility envelope on a value is anchored to a specific case, a specific session, a specific producer, and a specific layer's enum**, and it cannot drift. A user comparing two runs across sessions is doing that comparison as a user action, with the surface labelling each value with its own envelope; the product never silently aggregates across the boundary and presents the result as a single producer's emission.
   236	
   237	---
   238	
   239	## The honesty-contract envelope
   240	
   241	Every R3.0 producer emission — at every rung in whichever enum applies, including the `Unavailable` / blocked path — carries a `limitations[]` field. The field is an **array of machine-readable limitation codes** that name the honest scope caveats the producer attaches to the value. The field is always present (never absent/undefined), but it is **not** guaranteed non-empty on a clean emission: the R3.0E outcome classifier, for example, initializes `limitations = []` and only appends entries for specific conditions (invalid comparison, confounded control variables, data-quality issues) — a golden-path `confirmed` outcome with no caveats legitimately carries an empty `limitations[]`, not a synthesized baseline caveat. Some other producers do attach a standing scope caveat even on a clean run (e.g. "kinematic, confounded; not a professional-grade verdict" on a corner-delta computed from raw telemetry) — but that is a property of that specific producer's domain, not a blanket guarantee that `limitations[]` is never empty across all producers.
   242	
   243	Rules on `limitations[]`:
   244	
   245	1. **Append-only propagation across emissions.** A consumer of an emission appends its own limitations to the producer's; it never removes a producer's limitation. R3.0D's Engineer Brief carries the union of all upstream `limitations[]` lists from every evidence input that fed into the brief.
   246	2. **Stable codes.** Each limitation is a stable identifier (an enum value, not a free-form string), so that surfaces, exports, and migrations can translate them without re-parsing English. The catalogs of stable codes live alongside each producer module (R3.0C delta-metrics codes; R3.0D hypothesis-engine and Engineer Brief codes; R3.0E outcome-classifier and follow-up codes).
   247	3. **i18n via lookup, not via emission.** Producers emit codes; surfaces resolve codes to user-facing strings via the locale layer. A producer never emits a localised limitation string directly.
   248	4. **Crosses every boundary.** `limitations[]` survives R3.0B persistence (it is part of the frozen v1.4.0 schema), R3.0C export, R3.0D evidence-graph traversal, the R3.0E Outcome object, and R3.0F migration. There is no boundary at which it is dropped. (The R3.0E Timeline's events have no `limitations[]` field of their own — `contracts/r3.0e/case-timeline-contract.js`'s event shape is `{eventId, kind, createdAt, i18nKey, params}` — so there is nothing to "survive" a timeline append; an outcome's limitations live on the Outcome object, not on the timeline event that references it.)
   249	5. **No "limitations cleared" event.** A limitation is not a state; it is a fact about how the value was produced. Subsequent runs can produce new values with different limitations, but no run "clears" a prior value's limitations.
   250	
   251	`limitations[]` is the one field present on essentially every conclusion. The fail-closed-reason, evidence-reference, and next-validation-step concepts also exist, but their exact field names are **not** universal — there is no single `blockedReasons[]`/`evidenceRefs[]`/`nextValidationStep` triple shared by every producer. Concretely: R3.0C's credibility contract uses `blockedReasons[]`; the R3.0D Engineer Brief uses `cannotConcludeReasonCodes[]` for the fail-closed-reason concept, `evidenceSummary[]` (an array of `{nodeId, i18nKey, params}`) for the evidence-reference concept, and `nextValidationAction` (`{actionId, kind, i18nKey} | null`) for the next-validation-step concept; the R3.0C C6 export payload uses `blockers[]` and `nextValidationAction`. The R3.0E Outcome object has none of these fields — no `credibility`, `confidence`, `provenance`, or next-validation field at all (see `docs/r3-experiment-loop.md` for its real 13-key shape). "Honesty-contract envelope" describes a recurring pattern across producers, not one fixed field-name contract.
   252	
   253	---
   254	
   255	## Summary: how to read a credibility-tagged value correctly
   256	
   257	When an R3.0 surface renders a value, read it as a four-tuple plus the envelope, with the layer made explicit:
   258	
   259	- **Which enum applies.** R3.0C comparison-authority values draw from the six-value `CREDIBILITY_LADDER`. R3.0D evidence nodes draw from the four-value `EVIDENCE_CREDIBILITY` (for their `credibility` key). R3.0D conclusions (Engineer Brief) draw from the six-value `CONCLUSION_CREDIBILITY`. Separately, `'synthetic'` is also a value in the three-value `PROVENANCE` enum (`synthetic`/`real`/`unverified`), which exists as its own `provenance` key on **both** evidence nodes and the Engineer Brief — `credibility` and `provenance` are two distinct keys at both layers, not one name that changes meaning per layer. The synthetic-honesty enforcement constraint is specifically keyed on the `provenance` field, at whichever layer it appears.
   260	- **Provenance** (`real` / `synthetic` / `unverified`, at R3.0C and at the R3.0D conclusion layer) — what kind of data the value originated from. Synthetic origin is never stripped by any downstream transform and carries `LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY` through the chain.
   261	- **Limitations** — the honest caveats the producer attached, as stable codes.
   262	- **Blockers** — present only when the conclusion rung is `Unavailable`; the machine-readable reason the capability failed closed on this run.
   263	- **Evidence refs** — what raw inputs the value is anchored in.
   264	- **Next validation step** — what the user would have to do to upgrade the claim's credibility.
   265	
   266	A rung is **not** a trust score. It is a structural claim about how the value came to exist, drawn from a closed contract enum. The ladder in `credibility-and-trust.md` and the contract modules `contracts/r3.0c/credibility-contract.js` and `contracts/r3.0d/credibility-contract.js` together pin down what each rung in each enum permits a producer to claim and — more importantly — what it permits no one, downstream or upstream, to assume on the producer's behalf.
   267	
   268	Fail-closed is the default. Authority is re-derived, not presented. Producer attestation is verified, not trusted. The synthetic marker stays attached at whichever layer it was applied. Cross-case and cross-session never propagate authority. These five rules, applied at every R3.0 producer boundary, are what the R3.0 credibility model amounts to.
     1	# R3.0E Experiment Loop
     2	
     3	The experiment loop is the producer chain that turns a R3.0D Engineer Brief into a
     4	concrete, append-only record of *what was tried, what was applied, what happened
     5	next, and whether the next case could honestly tell us anything*. It is the part
     6	of the product where the system stops *recommending* and starts *bookkeeping*.
     7	This bookkeeping record is scoped to the experiment loop's own eight event kinds
     8	(see "Append-only Timeline" below); it does not log case-open, telemetry-import,
     9	reference-selection, comparison-execution, or free-text-note events.
    10	
    11	Like every other layer in R3.0, the loop is fail-closed and authoritative-only.
    12	It never decides on behalf of the engineer, never auto-applies a setup change,
    13	never auto-selects a reference, never overwrites the timeline, and never
    14	cross-pollinates cases. When a required input is missing, the corresponding
    15	capability is **blocked with a reason** — never approximated.
    16	
    17	This document covers the R3.0E surface only. R3.0F integrated-delivery concerns
    18	(migration, E2E, hardening) are described elsewhere.
    19	
    20	**Scope note.** R3.0E's own `featureRegistryActivationAllowed` flipped to `true`
    21	at `E5_ACTIVATION` (`governance/r3.0e/state.json`). The nav nodes
    22	`case:experiment_loop` / `case:case_timeline` (`NAV_NODES` in
    23	`renderer/js/feature-registry.js`) carry `availability: 'available'` — they
    24	are live in the navigation. The corresponding **features**
    25	`experiment_loop` / `case_timeline` (`FEATURES`, a separate keyspace) carry
    26	`availability: 'available_conditional'` — the R3.0E experiment viewmodel
    27	(`renderer/js/r3-0e-experiment-viewmodel.js`) returns an `unavailable`
    28	display state for a given case until that case actually carries an
    29	authoritative E3 outcome / E4 timeline projection. This is a per-case data
    30	gate, not a feature-registry gate. Note the shipped `renderer/index.html`
    31	does not yet load the R3.0E viewmodel scripts or render an Experiment
    32	Loop / Case Timeline pane — the nav nodes are live, but the pane content
    33	behind them is a downstream wire-in step; the viewmodel's display-state
    34	behavior is exercised today by its Node test suite, not by a shipped pane.
    35	
    36	## Producer chain: R3.0D engineer-brief → recommendation → experiment plan
    37	
    38	The chain that precedes an experiment plan spans two programs. **R3.0D owns the
    39	recommendation**; **R3.0E owns the experiment, its application, and its
    40	classification.** There is no R3.0E-side "recommendation producer" — the
    41	Recommendation shape itself is defined and validated by the non-production
    42	contract `contracts/r3.0d/recommendation-contract.js` and consumed inside
    43	R3.0D's own D4 Priority Engine / D5 Engineer Brief, not by an R3.0E module.
    44	
    45	Each stage consumes only the **authoritative** output of the previous stage;
    46	no stage will read caller-provided summary flags such as `eligible`,
    47	`confirmed`, `validated`, or `final`. Eligibility is re-derived from raw
    48	evidence on every run.
    49	
    50	The credibility ladder used throughout this document, top-to-bottom, is:
    51	**Physics > Model > Measured > Derived > Heuristic > Unavailable**. A stage may
    52	only *lower* the rung of an input as it propagates; it may never *raise* it.
    53	
    54	| Stage | Producer | Consumer input | Output | Credibility (ceiling) |
    55	|-------|----------|----------------|--------|-----------------------|
    56	| Evidence graph | R3.0D D2 | R3.0C comparison authority + R3.0B case-record | Authoritative evidence nodes | Measured / Derived |
    57	| Hypothesis | R3.0D D3 | Evidence graph (closure-private WeakSet verified) | Ranked hypotheses with reason-codes | Model / Derived / Heuristic |
    58	| Priority / Recommendation | R3.0D D4 | Verified hypotheses | Ranked candidates + a Recommendation (`contracts/r3.0d/recommendation-contract.js` shape: `priorityKey`, `applyMode` restricted to `driver_action`/`user_initiated`, no `auto_*`) | Heuristic |
    59	| Engineer Brief | R3.0D D5 | Priority list + evidence graph | Authoritative brief (read-only) | Heuristic |
    60	| Experiment Plan | R3.0E E1 | Engineer authors the plan, referencing the brief/recommendation by id | Experiment record (mutable via `create`/`update`/`get`/`list`/`remove`; the Timeline is the append-only audit trail — see below) | Derived |
    61	| Outcome Classification | R3.0E E3 | Experiment + applied change + follow-up comparison + control-variable observations | Outcome record (see "Outcome classifier" below) | Heuristic |
    62	
    63	*The rung shown is the maximum a stage can emit; actual rung is set per-run by
    64	the underlying input credibility.* A Priority or Engineer Brief node whose
    65	inputs are all Measured-rung still emerges at the **Heuristic** ceiling above,
    66	because ranking and prose-style framing are themselves heuristic operations on
    67	the underlying evidence. None of these rungs is a literal field stored on the
    68	emitted record — they are a documentation-level classification of each
    69	producer stage. The Outcome object specifically (see "Outcome classifier"
    70	below) has no `credibility` key in its 13-field shape at all; "Heuristic" here
    71	describes the *nature of the classification step* (a structured judgment over
    72	authoritative inputs via fixed thresholds, not a measured magnitude), not a
    73	runtime value the classifier emits.
    74	
    75	The Experiment record's `sourceHypothesisId` and `sourceRecommendationId`
    76	fields are **id-grammar-validated string references only** (`contracts/r3.0e/
    77	experiment-contract.js`) — there is no closure-private WeakSet that verifies
    78	an R3.0D-minted Recommendation or Engineer Brief object crossed the R3.0D→
    79	R3.0E boundary intact. The engineer is the one who reads the D5 brief and
    80	authors the E1 experiment record referencing it by id; R3.0E does not
    81	re-verify the brief's authoritative provenance at experiment-creation time.
    82	
    83	The chain has two non-negotiable properties:
    84	
    85	- **No runtime LLM authority.** Nothing in the chain calls an LLM at runtime to
    86	  decide a hypothesis, a recommendation, or an outcome. Reason-codes are
    87	  pre-enumerated; classifiers are deterministic.
    88	- **No causation upgrade.** A correlation in the evidence graph never becomes a
    89	  causation claim downstream. A hypothesis stays a hypothesis until an
    90	  experiment outcome is **confirmed** with same-case + same-session evidence,
    91	  and even then "confirmed" means *the predicted direction was observed under
    92	  the stated control variables*, not *the setup caused the lap time*, and never
    93	  *the driver caused the result*. Driver behaviour is not a vehicle finding.
    94	
    95	The recommendation stage emits **physical units only** (Nm/deg, N/mm, mm, %).
    96	Hardware clicks are never emitted — no validated per-car click→rate mapping
    97	exists, and fabricating one would silently bypass the credibility ladder.
    98	
    99	A Heuristic-rung recommendation is informational only; an experiment authored
   100	against it carries the Heuristic limitation verbatim. There is no separate
   101	`confidence` field on the Outcome object (see "Outcome classes" below) — the
   102	outcome classifier does not emit a confidence scalar at all, so there is no
   103	confidence cap to describe.
   104	
   105	## Experiment shape (E1 schema)
   106	
   107	The E1 store is the ledger of experiment plans. It is **mutable** — its
   108	`createExperimentStore` exposes `create(rec)`, `update(rec)`, `get(experimentId)`,
   109	`list()`, and `remove(experimentId)`. `update` enforces a stale-write check by
   110	comparing the persisted `createdAt` against the candidate; `remove` deletes both
   111	the payload and the index entry. The append-only ledger of *what the product
   112	told the engineer* lives in the Timeline store (see below), not here.
   113	
   114	Every experiment record has the following authoritative fields. `create`/`update`
   115	write the record into the backend's clone-on-write transaction (cloned, not frozen,
   116	at write time); `get` deep-freezes the record before returning it
   117	(`renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js`), so a caller reading a record back cannot mutate
   118	it in place.
   119	
   120	This table reflects the actual closed key set and validation in
   121	`contracts/r3.0e/experiment-contract.js`:
   122	
   123	| Field | Type | Validation | Meaning |
   124	|-------|------|-----------|---------|
   125	| `schemaVersion` | integer, currently `1` | Integer + future-schema check | Non-integer or `< 1` → `EXPERIMENT_INVALID`; `> 1` → `UNSUPPORTED_FUTURE_SCHEMA` (fail-closed). Omitting it fails validation — `Number.isInteger(undefined)` is false. |
   126	| `experimentId` | string, `exp_<16-32 hex>` | Grammar-checked | Stable identity. Never reused. |
   127	| `sourceCaseId` | string | Id-grammar-checked only | The case the experiment was authored against. No live cross-check against R3.0B at the contract layer. |
   128	| `sourceHypothesisId` | string | Id-grammar-checked only | Reference to the R3.0D D3 hypothesis being tested. Not re-verified against the evidence graph at write time. |
   129	| `sourceRecommendationId` | string | Id-grammar-checked only | Reference to the R3.0D D4/D5 recommendation that produced the plan. Not re-verified against an authoritative R3.0D producer set. |
   130	| `targetMetric` | non-empty string (≤512 bytes) | Length-checked only | Which metric the experiment is targeting (e.g. a R3.0C C5 delta-metric name). **Not an allowlist** — any non-empty string under the byte cap is accepted at this layer. |
   131	| `baselineValue` | finite number | `Number.isFinite` | The baseline reading from the source case. A plain number — no `{ value, units, credibility, provenance }` wrapper. |
   132	| `expectedDirection` | `'increase' \| 'decrease' \| 'no_change'` | Closed enum | The qualitative direction predicted by the model. (Not `"stay"` — the literal enum value is `'no_change'`.) |
   133	| `expectedMagnitudeRange` | `{ min: number, max: number }`, `min <= max` | Required, not nullable | A plain object with finite `min`/`max` where `min <= max`; `null` and non-plain values are rejected (`EXPERIMENT_EXPECTED_MAGNITUDE_RANGE_INVALID`). The contract does not enforce a closed key set on this nested object — only the top-level `EXPERIMENT_KEYS` list is closed (see below) — so an extra key such as a caller-supplied `units` is not itself rejected here; the documented/expected shape simply carries no `units` key. |
   134	| `setupChange` | plain object | `_isPlain` only | The setup lever(s) being moved. The contract checks it is a plain object; it does not enforce a specific physical-unit shape. |
   135	| `driverInstruction` | string \| `null` | Optional | Optional driver-facing note when non-null. |
   136	| `controlVariables` | array, ≤32 entries | `Array.isArray` + length cap only | Declared control variables. The E1 contract does not validate per-element shape; the E3 classifier reads each element's `.name` at runtime to compare against observed control variables. |
   137	| `validationPlan` | non-empty string (≤512 bytes) | Length-checked only | An i18n-key-style string naming the validation procedure. **Not a structured object.** |
   138	| `stopConditions` | array, 1–32 entries, each `{ i18nKey: string, params?: object }` | Shape-checked | Pre-enumerated guard conditions. The contract enforces the `{i18nKey, params}` shape and a non-empty `i18nKey`; it does not enforce a fixed catalogue of `i18nKey` values. |
   139	| `status` | enumerated | Closed enum | `'draft' \| 'planned' \| 'applied' \| 'completed' \| 'abandoned' \| 'invalid'`. The contract does not enforce transition order — that is left to the calling code. |
   140	| `followUpCaseIds` | array, ≤32 entries | `Array.isArray` + length cap only | Cases authored as the experimental follow-up. |
   141	| `outcome` | Outcome object \| `null` | Status-gated | Must be `null` while `status` is `'draft'`, `'planned'`, or `'applied'`. May be a plain object only when `status` is `'completed'`, `'abandoned'`, or `'invalid'` — this prevents a caller from attaching a classification result before the experiment has actually concluded. |
   142	| `createdAt` | ISO-8601 string | Non-empty string | Used as the stale-write guard on `update` (compared for exact equality, not freshness). |
   143	
   144	A field not in this list does **not** get silently dropped — the contract
   145	rejects the whole record fail-closed with `EXPERIMENT_INVALID`/
   146	`UNKNOWN_OWN_KEY` (`contracts/r3.0e/experiment-contract.js`'s
   147	`_hasOnlyAllowedKeys` check), and the store surfaces that as
   148	`R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_INVALID`. There is no silent-truncation path; an unknown
   149	field is a write failure, not a write-with-fields-removed.
   150	
   151	## Persistence semantics (R3.0E E2 stores)
   152	
   153	The E2 surface is a set of IndexedDB-backed stores reached through the R3.0B
   154	storage backend contract: `backend.transact({ stores, reads, compute })`. The
   155	production module is `renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js` (UMD export
   156	`R3_0E_Stores`), which composes five factories — `createExperimentStore`,
   157	`createOutcomeStore`, `createTimelineStore`, `createFollowUpLinkStore`, and
   158	`createStoreMetadata`. Per the D12/E1 ruling, R3.0E persistence lives in
   159	**separate versioned stores**; it does not extend the frozen R3.0B portable
   160	case-record schema body (which remains at v1.4.0).
   161	
   162	| Object store name | Keyed by | Holds |
   163	|-------------------|----------|-------|
   164	| `r3_0e_experiments` | `experimentId` | Experiment payload (E1 schemaVersion 1) |
   165	| `r3_0e_experimentsIndex` | `experimentId` | Summary index for `list()` |
   166	| `r3_0e_outcomes` | `outcomeId` | Outcome payload (schemaVersion 1) |
   167	| `r3_0e_outcomesIndex` | `outcomeId` | Summary index for `listForExperiment` |
   168	| `r3_0e_timelines` | `caseId` | One append-only timeline doc per case (schemaVersion 1) |
   169	| `r3_0e_followupLinks` | `linkId` | Follow-up link payload (schemaVersion 1) |
   170	| `r3_0e_followupLinksByCase` | `parentCaseId` | Reverse index, `{ parentCaseId, linkIds: Array<linkId> }` |
   171	| `r3_0e_storeMetadata` | constant key `__r3_0e_version` | Migration / schema-version marker |
   172	
   173	Mutability is **per store**, not global:
   174	
   175	- **`createExperimentStore`** — mutable. `create / update / get / list / remove`.
   176	  `update` enforces equality of the persisted `createdAt` against the candidate
   177	  and otherwise emits `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_STALE_WRITE`. `remove` deletes both
   178	  payload and index in one transaction.
   179	- **`createOutcomeStore`** — write-once-ish. Exposes only `create`, `get`, and
   180	  `listForExperiment`. There is no `update` and no `remove` — an outcome is
   181	  classified once.
   182	- **`createTimelineStore`** — **append-only**. Exposes only `getTimeline(caseId)`
   183	  and `appendEvent(caseId, event)`. No `update`, no `remove`. See the next
   184	  section for the append rules.
   185	- **`createFollowUpLinkStore`** — mutable in a constrained way. Exposes
   186	  `create`, `get`, `listForParent`, and `markParentStatus(linkId, newStatus)`.
   187	  `markParentStatus` accepts only `'present' | 'archived' | 'deleted'`; any
   188	  other value is rejected with `R3_0E_LINK_PARENT_STATUS_INVALID`.
   189	- **`createStoreMetadata`** — `readVersion()` and `writeVersion(versionMap)`,
   190	  used by the F1 migration runner.
   191	
   192	Across every store the contract is the same:
   193	
   194	- Every payload is validated by its E1 contract **before** write inside the
   195	  `compute()` of the `transact` call.
   196	- Every payload reached through a single-record read (`get()`; the per-link
   197	  reads inside `listForParent()`) is re-validated on read; a payload with a
   198	  `schemaVersion` greater than the current supported version (`1` for every
   199	  E2 store today) fails closed with `R3_0E_*_FUTURE_SCHEMA`. **Exception:**
   200	  `experimentStore.list()` and `outcomeStore.listForExperiment()`
   201	  (`renderer/js/r3-0e-stores.js:149`, `:213`) read directly from the
   202	  lightweight index stores and return those values as-is, with no
   203	  schema-version or shape check.
   204	- Persisted records carry **no runtime authority**: the closure-private
   205	  WeakSets that R3.0D and R3.0E E3 use to verify authoritative inputs cannot
   206	  survive a reload. Rehydration consumers must re-validate via the E1 contracts
   207	  before treating a payload as authoritative again.
   208	- Reason-codes are pre-enumerated. The full E2 set includes the
   209	  `R3_0E_EXPERIMENT_*` family (invalid, schema mismatch, id collision, missing,
   210	  future schema, stale write, corrupted), the `R3_0E_OUTCOME_*` family
   211	  (invalid, id collision, future schema, corrupted), the `R3_0E_TIMELINE_*`
   212	  family (future schema, corrupted, duplicate event, out of order, invalid),
   213	  the `R3_0E_LINK_*` family (invalid, id collision, future schema, corrupted,
   214	  missing, parent status invalid), plus `R3_0E_STORE_BACKEND_INVALID` and
   215	  `R3_0E_VERSION_INVALID`.
   216	
   217	## Apply change → follow-up case
   218	
   219	Applying a setup change is a **manual act by the engineer**. The system does
   220	not push a setup to a car, does not modify the source case, and does not silently
   221	clone evidence. The flow is:
   222	
   223	1. The engineer reads the experiment plan (E1 record).
   224	2. The engineer applies the change in the real world (or in the model, for a
   225	   model-only experiment).
   226	3. The engineer authors a **new follow-up case** in R3.0B persistence. The new
   227	   case is an `AnalysisCase` in its own right, with its own case id, its own
   228	   session, its own setup, and its own (optional) telemetry import.
   229	4. The engineer creates a **Follow-up Link** (E2) from the source case + source
   230	   experiment to the follow-up case.
   231	
   232	The follow-up case carries no implicit trust from the source case. It is loaded
   233	through the same R3.0B/R3.0C/R3.0D chain from scratch. R3.0B case-record schema
   234	is frozen at v1.4.0 and is not modified by R3.0E.
   235	
   236	The follow-up link itself has its own strict shape:
   237	
   238	| Field | Meaning |
   239	|-------|---------|
   240	| `schemaVersion` | Integer, currently `1`. |
   241	| `linkId` | Stable opaque id. |
   242	| `parentCaseId` | The case the experiment was authored against. |
   243	| `experimentId` | The experiment this follow-up is associated with. |
   244	| `followUpCaseId` | The case that holds the post-change session. |
   245	| `parentStatus` | `'present' \| 'archived' \| 'deleted'`. Mutated only via `markParentStatus(linkId, newStatus)`. |
   246	| `createdAt` | ISO-8601 timestamp. |
   247	
   248	The link store enforces:
   249	
   250	- **Path grammar** on `linkId`, `parentCaseId`, `followUpCaseId`, `experimentId`
   251	  (Codex E2-R1-03 closure).
   252	- **Re-validation per fetched link** in `listForParent` — a presence-only check
   253	  on the parent is never enough; every link is re-validated as it is fetched
   254	  (Codex E2-R1-02 closure).
   255	- **Reverse-index parent-membership** check (`r3_0e_followupLinksByCase` →
   256	  `r3_0e_followupLinks`), so a malformed write cannot leak follow-ups under the
   257	  wrong parent (Codex E2-R2-01 closure).
   258	- **Parent status mutation only via `markParentStatus(linkId, newStatus)`**,
   259	  with `newStatus` restricted to `'present' | 'archived' | 'deleted'`.
   260	
   261	There is intentionally no "merge" or "promote" operation. A follow-up case
   262	never *becomes* the source case, and the source case is never edited to reflect
   263	the follow-up's findings. The relationship is one-way and visible in the
   264	Timeline (below).
   265	
   266	## Case Link: cross-case comparison FORBIDDEN; follow-up links only
   267	
   268	The single most important rule of the experiment loop:
   269	
   270	**Comparison is SAME-CASE + SAME-SESSION only.**
   271	
   272	The R3.0C comparison authority will refuse to compute a delta between two laps
   273	that do not share a `caseId` and a `sessionId`. This is a hard, enforced
   274	boundary, not a guideline. The honest reasons are:
   275	
   276	- Tyre set, fuel mass, track surface, ambient conditions, driver state, and
   277	  sensor calibration differ across sessions in ways the product cannot
   278	  reconstruct after the fact.
   279	- A "cross-case PB" comparison would silently launder unverified provenance
   280	  into an apparently-measured delta.
   281	- The credibility ladder requires *Measured* to mean a real-time observation
   282	  inside a single, internally consistent telemetry window — not a stitched-up
   283	  artifact across two cases.
   284	
   285	Follow-up links are explicitly **not** comparison authority. A follow-up link
   286	expresses "this later case exists *because* of that earlier experiment". It
   287	does **not** authorize the comparison engine to delta the follow-up's laps
   288	against the source case's laps. Inside the follow-up case, the engineer
   289	performs a new, fresh, same-case + same-session comparison with an
   290	**explicitly user-selected** reference lap.
   291	
   292	Reference lap selection in the follow-up obeys the R3.0C C4 rule unchanged:
   293	**explicit user selection only**. There is no `fastest_valid`,
   294	`median`, `best_sector_composite`, or any other auto-pick. At the R3.0C
   295	comparison-authority layer, an unselected reference is rejected with
   296	`REFERENCE_NOT_SELECTED` (`contracts/r3.0c/reference-and-corner-contract.js`).
   297	At the R3.0E outcome-classifier layer (below), the follow-up's
   298	`hasExplicitReference !== true` flag folds into the `invalid_comparison` class
   299	with the limitation `OUTCOME_COMPARABILITY_INSUFFICIENT` — there is no
   300	`REFERENCE_LAP_NOT_SELECTED` code in the shipped R3.0E path.
   301	
   302	Delta sign in the follow-up case is, as always, `comparison − reference`.
   303	
   304	## Outcome classifier authoritative-only inputs
   305	
   306	The outcome classifier (`renderer/js/r3-0e-outcome-classifier.js`,
   307	authoritative entry `classifyOutcome(input, opts)`) is a deterministic
   308	function. It takes only the fields declared in its closed input-key set and
   309	emits a single classified Outcome. It never accepts a caller-supplied "final"
   310	outcome, never trusts an `eligible`/`confirmed` flag, and never reads a field
   311	outside its allowlisted input shape.
   312	
   313	The input wrapper has exactly five keys:
   314	
   315	1. **`experiment`** — the Experiment record (E1). The classifier requires it
   316	   to be deep-frozen and re-validates it against
   317	   `EXP.validateExperimentShape`. This is a **shape + immutability** check, not
   318	   a producer-identity WeakSet check — there is no cross-program authority
   319	   registry verifying the experiment was minted by a specific R3.0D/R3.0E
   320	   producer.
   321	2. **`appliedChange`** — `{ changeId, sourceExperimentId, appliedAt }`,
   322	   id-grammar- and timestamp-checked. The classifier does not re-derive what
   323	   was changed from `experiment.setupChange`; it only validates this small
   324	   envelope's shape and freshness.
   325	3. **`followUp`** — `{ followUpCaseId, parentCaseId, sessionId,
   326	   parentSessionId, hasExplicitReference, comparabilityScore }`. The
   327	   classifier derives `crossCase` (`followUp.parentCaseId !==
   328	   experiment.sourceCaseId`), `crossSession`
   329	   (`followUp.sessionId !== followUp.parentSessionId`), `noExplicitReference`
   330	   (`followUp.hasExplicitReference !== true`), and `lowComparability`
   331	   (`comparabilityScore < 0.5`) from this object. Any one of the four forces
   332	   `class = 'invalid_comparison'` — this is the highest-precedence outcome.
   333	4. **`observation`** — `{ observedDirection, observedMagnitude,
   334	   driverFeedback, dataQualityIssues, sideEffects, contradictingEvidenceIds,
   335	   supportingEvidenceIds }`. `observedDirection`/`observedMagnitude` drive the
   336	   confirmed/partially_confirmed/contradicted decision (see below);
   337	   `dataQualityIssues.length` above a fixed threshold (4) forces
   338	   `'inconclusive'`; a non-empty `contradictingEvidenceIds` forces
   339	   `'contradicted'` regardless of direction match.
   340	5. **`controlVariableObservations`** — an array of observed control-variable
   341	   readings. The classifier compares this against `experiment.controlVariables`
   342	   (declared by name): a declared variable missing from the observations, or
   343	   an observed variable flagged `withinRange !== true`, becomes a confounder
   344	   and forces `class = 'inconclusive_due_to_confounders'`.
   345	
   346	There is **no credibility-rung gate** in this classifier (no "Measured floor",
   347	no `Heuristic`/`Unavailable` rejection) and **no `cannotConclude` class** —
   348	that vocabulary does not exist in the shipped code. The six real classes are
   349	listed in the next section.
   350	
   351	Inputs the classifier explicitly **does not** read:
   352	
   353	- Any cross-case comparison (forbidden by construction; see above).
   354	- Any driver-behaviour observation as a vehicle finding. Driver behaviour
   355	  ≠ vehicle characteristic ≠ setup finding.
   356	- Any LLM-produced narrative. The classifier is reason-code-only.
   357	- Any caller-provided summary flag (`eligible`, `confirmed`, `class`, etc.).
   358	
   359	The output Outcome object has exactly thirteen keys, matching
   360	`contracts/r3.0e/outcome-contract.js`'s `OUTCOME_KEYS`:
   361	
   362	```
   363	{
   364	  schemaVersion,        // integer, currently 1
   365	  outcomeId,            // derived from experimentId + appliedChange.changeId
   366	  experimentId,         // echoes experiment.experimentId
   367	  class,                // one of six outcome classes (next section)
   368	  observedDirection,    // echoed from the observation input
   369	  observedMagnitude,    // echoed from the observation input
   370	  comparabilityScore,   // echoed from the followUp input
   371	  confounders,          // control-variable names that drifted/are missing (see step 5 above)
   372	  driverFeedback,       // echoed from the observation input
   373	  dataQualityIssues,    // echoed from the observation input
   374	  sideEffects,          // echoed from the observation input
   375	  limitations,          // reason codes accumulated during classification (always present, may be empty)
   376	  createdAt,            // ISO-8601, resolved from opts.clock / appliedChange.appliedAt / experiment.createdAt
   377	}
   378	```
   379	
   380	There is no `reasonCodes`, `supportingEvidenceIds`, `contradictingEvidenceIds`,
   381	`controlledVariableIntegrity`, `comparisonValidity`, `expectedVsObserved`,
   382	`cannotConclude`, `provenance`, or `generationToken` key on the output —
   383	those are either input-only fields (`supportingEvidenceIds`/
   384	`contradictingEvidenceIds` belong to the `observation` input) or do not exist
   385	in the shipped classifier at all.
   386	
   387	## Outcome classes: confirmed / partially_confirmed / contradicted / inconclusive / invalid_comparison / inconclusive_due_to_confounders
   388	
   389	The classifier emits exactly one of **six** classes
   390	(`contracts/r3.0e/outcome-contract.js`'s `OUTCOME_CLASS_ALLOWED`). The classes
   391	are evaluated in a fixed precedence order — when multiple conditions apply,
   392	the highest-precedence class wins:
   393	
   394	| Precedence | Class | When (from `renderer/js/r3-0e-outcome-classifier.js`) | What it does *not* mean |
   395	|---|-------|------|--------------------------|
   396	| 1 (highest) | `invalid_comparison` | `crossCase` (follow-up's `parentCaseId` ≠ experiment's `sourceCaseId`), OR `crossSession` (follow-up's `sessionId` ≠ `parentSessionId`), OR `noExplicitReference` (`hasExplicitReference !== true`), OR `lowComparability` (`comparabilityScore < 0.5`). | Does **not** mean the experiment failed — only that *no honest comparison was possible*. The setup change is not judged. |
   397	| 2 | `inconclusive_due_to_confounders` | A control variable declared in `experiment.controlVariables` is missing from the observed set, OR an observed control variable has `withinRange !== true`. | Does **not** mean the hypothesis is wrong — it means a variable the engineer promised to hold constant did not stay constant (or was never observed). |
   398	| 3 | `inconclusive` | `observation.dataQualityIssues.length` exceeds a fixed threshold (4), OR `observation.observedDirection === null`. | Does **not** mean "try again with more aggressive change" — it means the observation itself was too noisy or incomplete to classify. |
   399	| 4 | `contradicted` | `observation.contradictingEvidenceIds` is non-empty, OR the observed direction does not match `experiment.expectedDirection`. | Does **not** mean the hypothesis is universally false — only that the predicted direction was not observed under these controls in this session. |
